`docs/companions.md`:

```md
# Task routes and supplied source snapshots

Use the three independent repositories together when their tasks overlap; who owns what is stated once, in the [bundle README](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/Ultimate-AI-Starter-Bundle#ownership). This kit owns app/game binary analysis, extractors, instrumentation and REA.

Reverse THIS! contributes 7-Zip and DirectXTex; another forge may reference `sandbox/bin/7z.cmd` and the DirectXTex tools instead of installing a second copy. This kit consumes `UABS_PYTHON` and `UABS_TOOL_ROOTS` and may reuse an existing `um` installation; it does not install Skyrim tools or provider skills. The bundle's optional `reverse` pack calls this installer; a Reverse THIS! release does not require a bundle release.

Each installs independently and consumes verified existing installations. The workbench's standalone prerequisites are described in [standalone.md](standalone.md). Companion recommendations do not install a second provider stack, grant trust, register all MCP schemas or run an upstream account setup command.

Run `python scripts/check_companions.py` (source checkout) or the scheduled `companions` workflow to read the published bundle floor, asset template and Forge ownership link. It only reads; it never writes to either repository.

The supplied `Tools` folder was inspected on 2026-10-26. These are observations of those local snapshots, not claims that their folder names identify the latest upstream revision:

| Snapshot | Verified manifest/license | Task and reuse decision |
|---|---|---|
| `rea-main` | `package.json`: rea-agents 6.7.0; `LICENSE`: MIT, 2026 morluto | Reference source for REA. The owner kit uses integrity-pinned 6.8.0; do not replace it with this older snapshot. The official [6.8.0 npm record](https://registry.npmjs.org/rea-agents/6.8.0) identifies the package/integrity. No v6.8.0 Git tag or gitHead was present in the checked upstream metadata. |
| `universal-modder-main` | `pyproject.toml`: universal-modder 0.2.0, Python >=3.10; `LICENSE`: MIT | [Universal Modder](https://github.com/rehan-remade/universal-modder) supplies game reconnaissance, field notes and asset/capture workflows. Reuse an installed `um`; the bundle already ships this version. Follow its upstream isolated installation when the bundle is absent. Paid generation is optional. |
| `CLI-Anything-main` | `LICENSE`: Apache-2.0 | [CLI-Anything](https://github.com/HKUDS/CLI-Anything) supplies task-specific application CLI harnesses. Select the relevant Blender, Godot, graphics, browser or other harness when it fills the actual task; consult its hub and harness prerequisites. This source collection is not another binary analysis toolkit. |
| `context-mode-main` | `package.json`: context-mode 1.0.169, Node >=22.5.0, Elastic-2.0; `LICENSE`: Elastic License 2.0 | [Context Mode](https://github.com/mksglu/context-mode) is an optional agent-context/plugin workflow. Evaluate it against the host's existing context tools and provider configuration. Its ELv2 terms are distinct from MIT; do not merge/relicense its source into the workbench or replace existing hooks silently. |

No snapshot was vendored wholesale. MIT or Apache-2.0 files can be considered individually when a concrete missing feature warrants reuse, retaining their actual notices and exact revision provenance. The observations above do not grant rights over third-party dependencies, target games or extracted assets. Context Mode remains a separately licensed upstream component. Prefer owner installers and existing installed commands to copying redundant source trees.

```

`docs/coverage.md`:

```md
# Coverage and remaining routes

Reverse THIS! supplies local analysis tools. [Universal Modder](https://github.com/rehan-remade/universal-modder) supplies the game workflow and field notes. [Ultimate AI Starter Bundle](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/Ultimate-AI-Starter-Bundle) supplies provider setup, skills and scoped MCPs, and already includes `um` and the shared Universal Modder skill. Reuse those instead of registering duplicate plugins or fal servers. This installer does not edit provider configuration or trust.

The catalog is an install plan; `sandbox/installed.json` records what a run installed. A successful install proves neither target compatibility nor a working modification. Verify the chosen command and then one small target change.

## Included routes

| Target or job | Included tools | Limit to verify |
|---|---|---|
| Native PE, ELF and other supported executable formats | Ghidra, Detect It Easy, ImHex, PE-bear, capa | Decompiled C is an approximation; symbols, types and build details may be absent. Confirm capability-rule matches. |
| Windows debugging and instrumentation | x64dbg, Frida, MinHook, SafetyHook | Architecture and target version matter; hooks require target-specific evidence. |
| .NET and Unity Mono | ILSpy, ilspycmd, dnSpyEx, BepInEx 5 | Current [ilspycmd](https://www.nuget.org/packages/ilspycmd) targets .NET 10; the installer provisions its SDK when absent, including on UABS setups with only older .NET versions. Obfuscation, runtime changes and missing dependencies can prevent a rebuild. |
| Unity IL2CPP and assets | Il2CppDumper, AssetRipper, UABEA, MelonLoader, UnityExplorer variants | Metadata does not recover native method bodies. Choose plugins for the actual loader/runtime. |
| Android DEX/APK, Java bytecode | JADX CLI and GUI; Apktool | Source reconstruction is incomplete in some cases. Apktool decodes/rebuilds resources and smali; signing and device tooling are separate. |
| PyInstaller-packaged Python | pyinstxtractor-ng | Extracts files and bytecode. It does not guarantee Python source decompilation or support arbitrary Nuitka/native extensions. |
| Electron archives | Electron ASAR (`asar`) | Lists, extracts and repacks archives. Minification, absent source maps, native modules and app integrity/signatures need separate work. |
| WebAssembly | wasm-tools | Inspect, validate, assemble and disassemble modules/components; this does not recreate original source or build tooling. |
| Programmable PE/ELF/Mach-O parsing and patching | LIEF Python library | Use the isolated `sandbox/tools/lief/Scripts/python.exe`; this library has no CLI shim. Verify modified files before running them. |
| Undocumented binary formats | ImHex, Kaitai Struct compiler | Write and validate a format specification; generated parsers need a language runtime. Kaitai is not an automatic format detector. |
| Generic archives | 7-Zip | Proprietary packing and custom compression can require a format-specific tool. |
| Nintendo Switch NSP/XCI/NCA/RomFS | nstool | Lists and extracts containers; a matching keyset/ticket is required. Verify the title ID and build before extracting. |
| Switch textures and common formats | Switch Toolbox | BNTX and many common Switch formats; TRMDL viewing is incomplete for Scarlet/Violet (upstream issue #658). Use the Blender importer for Pokémon models. |
| League of Legends WAD archives | wadtools | Lists and extracts `.wad.client` chunks; path recovery scans `.bin` files and may update its hash-table cache. Read-only extraction does not install a client mod. |
| Unreal, Godot, Frostbite | FModel, repak, retoc, UAssetGUI, UE4SS; GDRE and Godot; Frosty tools | Check engine/game versions. A source archive or loader is not a supported executable for every game. |
| Graphics and textures | RenderDoc, DirectXTex | API/driver support and capture compatibility vary; this is not every vendor's profiling suite. |
| Binary comparison | Ghidra Version Tracking, ImHex, `rz-diff` | Compare the same architecture and record target hashes; generated names are not stable identifiers. |
| Packed executables | UPX | Only UPX-packed files unpack cleanly; other packers need their own unpacker. |
| Stripped Go binaries | GoReSym | Symbol and build-info recovery depends on the Go version and build flags. |
| Rule-based scanning | YARA (`yara`, `yarac`) | Rules are yours to write; a match is a lead, not proof of behavior. |
| Import and dependency view | Dependencies (CLI and GUI) | Resolution can differ from what the loader does at runtime. |
| Live process, handle and module inspection | System Informer | Needs the same privilege as the target; kernel protections can block it. |
| Obfuscated or runtime strings | FLOSS | Recovered strings can be decoys; emulation may not reach every code path. |
| Scripted native analysis | Rizin (`rizin`, `rz-bin`, `rz-diff`, `rz-asm`) | Command syntax differs from radare2; check the installed version's help. |
| PE fingerprint and proxy planning | `pe-recon` | JSON only; confirm the runtime import set before choosing a proxy DLL. |
| AOB signature generation | `aob-gen` | Validate uniqueness in two builds; a signature is not a patch. |
| Ghidra MCP operation | `ghidra-mcp-up` and the Ghidra recipe | 8080 is the Ghidra plugin, 8081 is the MCP bridge; neither is a decompiler by itself. |
| JVM decompilation, second opinion | Vineflower | Compare with JADX output; neither restores the original source. |
| APK signing | uber-apk-signer | You supply the key; a debug key is not a release identity. |
| Scripted Unity asset work | UnityPy | Type trees are Unity-version specific; this is a library, not a GUI. |
| Source models and maps | Crowbar, BSPSource | Format and game support vary; materials and mounts are separate. |
| Source 2 assets | Source 2 Viewer (GUI and CLI) | Supports current VPK/resource versions; not every resource type exports. |
| REDengine archives and assets | WolvenKit (GUI and CLI) | Game-version support and archive format changes matter. |
| FromSoftware mods | Mod Engine 2, Smithbox | Per-game offsets and regulation versions; online/anti-cheat use needs the current policy check in `encyclopedia/anticheat-risk.md`. |
| GameMaker games | UndertaleModTool (GUI and CLI) | `data.win` version support varies; not every runner build is covered. |
| Total War packs | RPFM | Schemas are per game; verify the pack version before writing. |
| App behavior recon and clean-room rebuild | replica-skill (`replica-recon`, `-diff`, `-test`, others) | MIT Claude skills with stdlib Python helpers. They work from public pages, screenshots and your own account; they do not analyze binaries. The installer resolves the current default branch to a full immutable commit, records that archive identity and its SHA-256, and reuses the recorded version with `-NoUpdate`; review the selected source before use. Rebuilding features is not a licence to copy code, assets or branding. |

The game-specific SDKs, fighting-game tools and art editors remain in the catalog. They do not extend generic app decompilation. Bethesda and Skyrim tooling is owned by [Skyrim Forge](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/SkyrimForge); this kit does not duplicate xEdit, LOOT or the Creation Kit.

## Added tool sources and runtimes

| Tool | Primary upstream | Runtime and license |
|---|---|---|
| JADX | [skylot/jadx](https://github.com/skylot/jadx) | Apache-2.0; Java 11+ upstream, JDK 25 shared by this kit. |
| capa | [mandiant/capa](https://github.com/mandiant/capa), [installation guide](https://github.com/mandiant/capa/blob/master/doc/installation.md), [released rules](https://github.com/mandiant/capa-rules) | Apache-2.0 code and rules; `flare-capa` in a venv requires Python 3.10+. Released rules install as `capa-rules` and the signature-bearing release source archive as `capa-source`, including with `-Only capa`. The shim supplies both paths; the separate malware testfiles submodule is not downloaded. |
| Kaitai compiler | [kaitai-io/kaitai_struct_compiler](https://github.com/kaitai-io/kaitai_struct_compiler), [runtime licenses](https://kaitai.io/#licensing) | GPL-3.0-or-later compiler; Java 8+ upstream, JDK 25 shared here. Generated parsers need the selected language runtime under its own license. |
| pyinstxtractor-ng | [pyinstxtractor/pyinstxtractor-ng](https://github.com/pyinstxtractor/pyinstxtractor-ng) | GPL-3.0; standalone executable, no matching target Python version needed for extraction. |
| wasm-tools | [bytecodealliance/wasm-tools](https://github.com/bytecodealliance/wasm-tools) | Triple licensed under Apache-2.0, Apache-2.0 with LLVM exception, and MIT; standalone Windows x64 zip. |
| Apktool | [iBotPeaches/Apktool](https://github.com/iBotPeaches/Apktool), [installation guide](https://apktool.org/docs/install/), [decode/build options](https://apktool.org/docs/cli-parameters/) | Apache-2.0; Java 8+ confirmed for v3.0.3, JDK 25 shared here. Supply Android framework files if the target needs them. Case-distinct APK paths need a case-sensitive work directory; the installer does not change that Windows setting. |
| Electron ASAR | [electron/asar](https://github.com/electron/asar) | MIT; `@electron/asar` requires Node 22.12+ and npm. Local prefix with package lifecycle scripts disabled. |
| LIEF | [lief-project/LIEF](https://github.com/lief-project/LIEF), [Python package metadata](https://pypi.org/project/lief/) | Apache-2.0; Python 3.9+ in a separate venv. Use the installed version's API. |
| UPX | [upx/upx](https://github.com/upx/upx) | GPL-2.0-or-later with the UPX special exception; standalone Windows x64 zip. |
| YARA | [VirusTotal/yara](https://github.com/VirusTotal/yara) | BSD-3-Clause; standalone Windows x64 zip with `yara64` and `yarac64`. |
| GoReSym | [mandiant/GoReSym](https://github.com/mandiant/GoReSym) | MIT; standalone Windows executable. |
| Dependencies | [lucasg/Dependencies](https://github.com/lucasg/Dependencies) | MIT; x64 release zip with CLI and GUI. |
| Rizin | [rizinorg/rizin](https://github.com/rizinorg/rizin) | LGPL-3.0; static Windows x64 zip. |
| System Informer | [winsiderss/systeminformer](https://github.com/winsiderss/systeminformer) | MIT; portable bin zip, amd64 tree. |
| FLOSS | [mandiant/flare-floss](https://github.com/mandiant/flare-floss) | Apache-2.0; `flare-floss` in a venv, Python 3.10+. |
| UnityPy | [K0lb3/UnityPy](https://github.com/K0lb3/UnityPy) | MIT; `UnityPy` in a venv, Python 3.8+. |
| Vineflower | [Vineflower/vineflower](https://github.com/Vineflower/vineflower) | Apache-2.0; JAR, Java 17+, JDK 25 shared here. |
| uber-apk-signer | [patrickfav/uber-apk-signer](https://github.com/patrickfav/uber-apk-signer) | Apache-2.0; JAR, Java 8+, JDK 25 shared here. |
| WolvenKit | [WolvenKit/WolvenKit](https://github.com/WolvenKit/WolvenKit) | GPL-3.0; self-contained Windows zip for GUI and CLI. |
| Mod Engine 2 | [soulsmods/ModEngine2](https://github.com/soulsmods/ModEngine2) | MIT; Windows x64 zip. |
| Smithbox | [vawser/Smithbox](https://github.com/vawser/Smithbox) | MIT; self-contained Windows zip. |
| Crowbar | [ZeqMacaw/Crowbar](https://github.com/ZeqMacaw/Crowbar) | CC BY-SA 3.0; 7-Zip archive containing `Crowbar.exe`. |
| BSPSource | [ata4/bspsrc](https://github.com/ata4/bspsrc) | Public domain; JAR-only zip, Java 24+, JDK 25 shared here. |
| Source 2 Viewer | [ValveResourceFormat/ValveResourceFormat](https://github.com/ValveResourceFormat/ValveResourceFormat) | MIT; single-file GUI exe and Windows x64 CLI zip. |
| UndertaleModTool | [UnderminersTeam/UndertaleModTool](https://github.com/UnderminersTeam/UndertaleModTool) | GPL-3.0; self-contained Windows GUI and CLI zips. |
| RPFM | [Frodo45127/rpfm](https://github.com/Frodo45127/rpfm) | MIT; Windows x64 zip with `rpfm_ui.exe`. |
| Capstone | [capstone-engine/capstone](https://github.com/capstone-engine/capstone) | BSD-3-Clause; Python package in a venv; used by `aob-gen`. |
| Local kit helpers | `kit/` in this repository | MIT; Python standard library, plus Capstone for `aob-gen`. |

Selecting `capa`, `capa-rules` or `capa-source` for installation updates the entire matched capa group in one transaction. Plan and Doctor still list/check the individual catalog entries.

`Install-Modkit.ps1 -Doctor` checks records and required files. It does not import every library or prove target compatibility. For LIEF, run `.\sandbox\tools\lief\Scripts\python.exe -c "import lief; print(lief.__version__)"` and then a small parse on a copied target. Java, Python, .NET and Node prerequisites on the host do not automatically become available in a Windows Sandbox guest.

Installer diagnostics are in `sandbox/log/modkit.log`; `-DebugLog` enables detailed package/archive output. Updates retain previous payloads in `sandbox/backups` and save a pre-update manifest. Keep the rollback copy until the new tool works on your target.

## Optional routes, not installed by this kit

These are primary upstream entry points checked on 2026-10-11. Resolve current versions and prerequisites before use. Put portable files under `sandbox/manual/<name>` or use a project-local environment. Do not assume a shim exists.

| Gap | Upstream route | Setup and scope |
|---|---|---|
| Android signing and device debugging | [Android SDK Build Tools](https://developer.android.com/tools/releases/build-tools), [SDK Platform Tools](https://developer.android.com/tools/releases/platform-tools) | `uber-apk-signer` covers signing and zipalignment. The separate SDK is still needed for `adb`, device work and Google's own `apksigner`; licenses apply. |
| Newer Unity IL2CPP metadata or a second analysis route | [Cpp2IL preview 2022.1.0-pre-release.21](https://github.com/SamboyCoding/Cpp2IL/releases/tag/2022.1.0-pre-release.21) | MIT; preview binaries published 2026-02-22 include a Windows EXE and .NET Framework 4.7.2 ZIP. Choose the correct architecture/runtime and verify target metadata support. No preview is automatically installed. |
| Source 2 tools not covered by the viewer | [Source 2 SDK / ValveResourceFormat source](https://github.com/ValveResourceFormat/ValveResourceFormat) | Build from source when a resource type or game version is newer than the released binaries. |
| RE Engine mods | [REFramework](https://github.com/praydog/REFramework) | MIT; releases are one ZIP per game (RE2, RE3, RE4, RE7, RE8, DD2, MH Wilds, SF6 and others). Pick the game's asset; a single pattern cannot select all of them. |
| GTA V modding | [CodeWalker](https://github.com/dexyfex/CodeWalker) | The repository publishes no GitHub release. Use the author's published build; verify its provenance. |
| Modern Unity asset/bundle editing | [UABEANext](https://github.com/nesrak1/UABEANext) | MIT; active UABEA fork with dock support. It has no stable GitHub release; use the nightly build linked from its README or build from source. Keep the installed UABEA build for stable edits. |
| Pokémon Switch model conversion | [Pokémon Switch Model Importer/Exporter for Blender](https://github.com/ChicoEevee/Pokemon-Switch-Model-Importer-Blender) | Imports TRMDL/TRMSH/TRMBF and animations in Blender 4.x/5.x; no release binary. Z-A models were not working per its README on 2026-11-01. Verify one model before converting a roster. |
| Pokémon Sword/Shield data editing | [pkNX](https://github.com/kwsch/pkNX) | GPL-3.0; Sword/Shield editing, Scarlet/Violet and Z-A dump-only in the current README. No GitHub release asset; build from source. |
| Legacy Switch archive/text editing | [Kuriimu2](https://github.com/FanTranslatorsInternational/Kuriimu2) | GPL-3.0; last release 1.2.1 (2021) requires .NET Core 3.1. Use only when its archive plugins match the target; not the default route. |
| League client mods and skins | [LTK Manager](https://github.com/LeagueToolkit/ltk-manager) | GPL-3.0; the official LeagueToolkit mod manager/IDE, not a portable extractor. It installs a client-side mod setup; this kit does not auto-install it. Authorised cosmetic use only; Vanguard and Riot's terms still apply. |
| Naruto Storm CPK/XFBIN editing | [CriPakTools](https://github.com/esperknight/CriPakTools), [NSC-Toolbox](https://github.com/TheLeonX/NSC-Toolbox) | CPK list/extract/replace and Storm-specific XFBIN editing. No declared licences and no permissive packaged release for the CPK tool, so both are manual-only; back up archives and test one change. |
| Xbox 360 XEX/PPC static recompilation | [ReXGlue SDK](https://github.com/rexglue/rexglue-sdk) | BSD-3-Clause-style; a per-game SDK, not a push-button converter. The v0.10.0 Windows ZIP is 44.7 MB, but its official `rexruntime.dll` is a known Defender heuristic false positive (28/71 engines, upstream issue #485, 2026-10-07), so this kit does not auto-install it. Rebuild from the v0.10.0 tag or use the reported-clean nightly after checking hashes; it needs VS 2022 C++/Clang 20, CMake 3.25+ and Ninja, and unresolved functions and kernel imports need manual work. Own game files only. |
| Rizin GUI | [Cutter](https://github.com/rizinorg/cutter) | GPL-3.0; the `Cutter-v*-Windows-x86_64.zip` GUI over the installed `rizin` CLI. Optional when Ghidra already covers GUI analysis. |
| Second native decompiler | [RetDec](https://github.com/avast/retdec) | MIT; last release v5.0 (2023) ships a Windows 7z. Ghidra and Rizin are the maintained defaults; use RetDec only for a target where its output helps. |
| JVM bytecode editing | [Recaf](https://github.com/Col-E/Recaf/releases/tag/4.0.0-alpha) | MIT; 4.x is explicitly a preview despite GitHub marking its tag as a normal release. Windows artifact `recaf-4x-alpha-win-86-x64.jar`, Java 22+. Do not relabel it stable. |
| File, registry and process activity | [Microsoft Process Monitor](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sysinternals/downloads/procmon) | Separate Sysinternals license and first-run acceptance; monitoring can require elevation and a driver. Current 4.11 requires Windows 11; it is not part of this kit's Windows 10 baseline. |
| Windows dumps and system debugging | [WinDbg](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/debugger/) | Microsoft-managed installation and symbol setup; not a portable payload here. Preserve private dumps and symbol caches outside source. |
| HTTP(S)/WebSocket behavior | [mitmproxy](https://github.com/mitmproxy/mitmproxy), [Wireshark](https://www.wireshark.org/download.html) | Separate installation/runtime requirements. HTTPS interception needs an explicit target-specific certificate setup; packet capture may need Npcap. Do not silently install certificates, drivers or modify system proxy settings. |
| Game-specific archives not handled by 7-Zip | [QuickBMS](https://aluigi.altervista.org/quickbms.htm) and the game's format documentation | Not automatically downloaded; check the downloaded archive's terms. Scripts are executable instructions and format/version specific; inspect them and test with copies. |
| Older Unreal and target-specific UE4 resource extraction | [UE Viewer / UModel](https://www.gildor.org/en/projects/umodel) | Supports older Unreal engines and many UE4 games; check its compatibility table for the target. The official page says its license is not determined, so it is a manual route rather than a redistributed kit payload. |
| Actual isolation | [Windows Sandbox](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/application-security/application-isolation/windows-sandbox/), a separate VM | `Start-IsolatedLab.ps1 -Plan` previews this kit's configuration. Launch requires the Windows feature on a supported Pro/Enterprise/Education edition; Windows Home needs a separate VM. Tools and optional input are read-only, only `sandbox/work/isolated` is shared writable, and networking/clipboard are disabled. No target starts at logon. Host runtimes are not copied; use a prepared VM when the route needs them. |

UABS's existing browser, source-navigation and editor routes remain useful after source is available. They are not additional decompilers. Generic development runtimes, compilers, Android SDKs and vendor GPU suites are not all bundled here.

The full Pokémon Switch extraction, modding and native-port route is in [pokemon-switch.md](pokemon-switch.md). League's WAD/LTK route is in [league.md](league.md), and the Naruto CPK/UE4 route is in [naruto-storm.md](naruto-storm.md).

## Recovery, reimplementation and publication

Record target hashes, architecture, runtime and dependency versions before analysis. Keep originals and a rollback copy. Work on copies under `sandbox/work/<target>`; inspect one change before applying it more broadly. Logs, dumps and captures can contain private data.

Recovered source can be incomplete or inaccurate. Missing server code, assets, symbols, keys and build systems do not become available because a binary was decompiled. For a replacement implementation, document observed inputs/outputs and verify behavior against the original target.

Technical access does not change copyright or licensing. The kit's MIT license covers its own code, not third-party tools or analyzed targets. Keep tool licenses, target provenance and permission to publish separate. Do not call a target open source merely because extracted or reconstructed code is readable, and keep proprietary target files and private analysis out of releases.

No finite catalog can guarantee every app, engine, obfuscator or future format. Add a tool when a verified target needs it and its artifact, runtime, license and rollback path are known.

```

`docs/currency.md`:

```md
# What is in the kit, and what was left out

The installer resolves the current official release at install time. This page is the cut list. It names projects, not pins, except where the pin itself is the thing being rejected.

## Installed on purpose

- Ghidra's current public release, with JDK 25. Headless is `analyzeHeadless`.
- ImHex's Windows portable x64 zip. Not the NoGPU build, not the arm64 build, not the MSI.
- Detect It Easy's win64 portable zip from `horsicq/DIE-engine`. `diec` is the CLI. `detectiteasy.com` is not an official download.
- x64dbg's current snapshot zip, the x64 debugger. Not the symbols zip.
- PE-bear's Qt 6 x64 Windows zip.
- dnSpyEx's `dnSpy-net-win64` zip. Not the .NET Framework build.
- ILSpy's self-contained Windows x64 zip, plus the [`ilspycmd` dotnet tool](https://www.nuget.org/packages/ilspycmd). Current ilspycmd 11.2 targets .NET 10; its installer checks for the SDK/runtime and uses the official `Microsoft.DotNet.SDK.10` winget package when absent. Older UABS .NET prerequisites alone do not satisfy this tool. The non-self-contained `ILSpy_windows_*` zip needs a .NET runtime and is not installed.
- AssetRipper's Windows x64 zip.
- Il2CppDumper's Windows zip (`Il2CppDumper-win-`), not the net6 or net7 framework-dependent zips.
- FModel's current zip.
- repak and retoc Windows x64 CLI zips. repak is `.pak`. retoc is IoStore. Neither replaces the other.
- UAssetGUI's published exe, and the current DirectXTex `texconv`, `texassemble` and `texdiag` exes. Not the arm64 exes.
- UABEA's Windows zip. That is the Unity asset editor that still publishes a binary.
- BepInEx 5's current Windows x64 release, for Mono games.
- MelonLoader's current x64 zip, for Mono and IL2CPP.
- UnityExplorer builds from the yukieiji fork: BepInEx 5 Mono, BepInEx 6 Mono, and MelonLoader IL2CPP. Those are plugins, not loaders.
- UE4SS from the `experimental-latest` release, asset `UE4SS_*.zip` only. Not `zDEV`, not `zCustomGameConfigs`, not `zMapGenBP`.
- RenderDoc's stable portable x64 zip from `renderdoc.org`, using the version of the current GitHub release tag. Not the nightly. Not the MSI.
- MinHook's bin zip, for C. SafetyHook's amalgamated Zydis sources, for new C++.
- Frida tools in a venv under `sandbox/tools/frida`.
- [JADX](https://github.com/skylot/jadx)'s cross-platform `jadx-<version>.zip`, containing both CLI and GUI batch launchers. The separate Windows GUI zip would omit the CLI. Upstream requires Java 11+; this kit shares its JDK 25. Apache-2.0.
- [capa](https://github.com/mandiant/capa)'s official `flare-capa` Python package in its own venv, Python 3.10+. [Released capability rules](https://github.com/mandiant/capa-rules) install as `capa-rules`; capa's release source archive installs as `capa-source` to supply `sigs/`. Both dependencies install even with `-Only capa`; the shim supplies `-r` and `-s` with those directories. The source archive does not include the separate malware testfiles submodule. This follows the [upstream package installation route](https://github.com/mandiant/capa/blob/master/doc/installation.md), rather than using the packed standalone executable. capa and its rules are Apache-2.0. Matches are leads to confirm, not proof that code executes a behavior.
- [Kaitai Struct compiler](https://github.com/kaitai-io/kaitai_struct_compiler)'s universal `kaitai-struct-compiler-<version>.zip`, including the Windows batch launcher. Upstream requires Java 8+; this kit shares its JDK 25. GPL-3.0-or-later compiler; generated parsers need the target language's separate runtime with its own license.
- [pyinstxtractor-ng](https://github.com/pyinstxtractor/pyinstxtractor-ng)'s standalone Windows `pyinstxtractor-ng.exe`. No local Python installation or matching target Python version is required for extraction. Decompiling extracted bytecode is a separate, version-dependent task. GPL-3.0.
- [wasm-tools](https://github.com/bytecodealliance/wasm-tools)'s `wasm-tools-<version>-x86_64-windows.zip`. One self-contained CLI for inspection, validation, text/binary conversion and component work. Triple licensed under Apache-2.0, Apache-2.0 with LLVM exception, and MIT.
- [Apktool](https://github.com/iBotPeaches/Apktool)'s `apktool_<version>.jar`, stored without extraction and launched with `java -jar`. Java 8+ is confirmed by the [v3.0.3 build configuration](https://github.com/iBotPeaches/Apktool/blob/v3.0.3/build.gradle.kts) and the released JAR's Java 8 class version; this kit shares its JDK 25. Apache-2.0. See the current [decode/build CLI options](https://apktool.org/docs/cli-parameters/); Android signing and device tools are separate.
- [Electron ASAR](https://github.com/electron/asar)'s `@electron/asar` npm package under `sandbox/tools/asar`, without lifecycle scripts or a global installation. Node 22.12+ and npm are required. MIT.
- [LIEF](https://github.com/lief-project/LIEF)'s `lief` Python package in its own venv under `sandbox/tools/lief`. Python 3.9+ is required. Use that environment's Python to import the library; there is no LIEF CLI shim. Apache-2.0.
- Frosty Editor and Frosty Mod Manager from the current stable release (`FrostyEditor.zip` and `FrostyModManager.zip`). Not the bundled prereq installers. Those stay inside the unpacked zip and are not shims.
- Godot's current stable mono win64 zip. The shim is the console executable. Not the win32 zip, not the arm64 zip, not the non-mono `.exe.zip`.
- GDRE Tools' Windows zip, for packed Godot games.
- IKEMEN Go's Windows zip. Not the Android, Linux, or macOS zips.
- Pixelorama's Windows 64-bit zip. Not the 32-bit zip and not the setup exe.
- 7-Zip's current x64 MSI. `msiexec /a` extracts it into `sandbox/tools/7zip`. It is not installed for the machine. Not the arm64 build, not the 32-bit package, not the exe installer. `7z.exe` plus `7z.dll` is what unpacks the two tools below.
- LDtk's current Windows installer. 7-Zip extracts the 64-bit `app-64.7z` payload only. The installer is not executed. Not the 32-bit payload, not the macOS or Ubuntu zips.
- OpenBOR's current Windows rar. Not the Android apk and not the Linux AppImages.
- Fighter Factory's current official Windows portable zip. The download page is read at install time and the first Windows portable topic is the one that is downloaded. The shim is x64 `ffs.exe`. Not `ffs.qt4.exe`, not the 32-bit build, and not the installer topic.
- HD2Runtime's current SDK, runtime, example projects, and mod template zips, as four separate tools. HD2Runtime ModBuilder's current Windows x64 zip. The shim is `HD2RuntimeModBuilder.exe`, not the `HD2RuntimeGUI.exe` compatibility copy. The community SDK zip. Bingus Shared Loader's loader zip, not its source zip.
- R2API source for the current release tag. That repository publishes source, not a bench executable.
- Sleeping Dogs TheoryEngine source, SDK source (the repository's default branch), and the SDK playground examples. These trees contain no game executable.
- UPX's `upx-<version>-win64.zip`; the `upx` shim unpacks a target. Only UPX-packed files are supported by UPX.
- YARA's `yara-<version>-<build>-win64.zip`; `yara` and `yarac` are the 64-bit CLIs.
- GoReSym's `GoReSym-windows.zip`; one static executable.
- Dependencies' `Dependencies_x64_Release.zip`, CLI and GUI. Not the debug or x86 build.
- Rizin's `rizin-windows-static-v<version>.zip`; the static build has no separate VC runtime requirement. Not the installer or the shared build.
- System Informer's `systeminformer-<version>-bin.zip`, amd64 tree. Not the setup exe.
- FLOSS (`flare-floss`) in its own venv, Python 3.10+.
- UnityPy in its own venv, Python 3.8+; a library with no shim.
- Vineflower's `vineflower-<version>.jar`, not the slim jar. Java 17+; JDK 25 is shared.
- uber-apk-signer's `uber-apk-signer-<version>.jar`. Java 8+; JDK 25 is shared.
- WolvenKit's `WolvenKit-<version>.zip` and `WolvenKit.Console-<version>.zip`; GUI and CLI are separate catalog entries. Not the setup exe and not the Linux console build.
- Mod Engine 2's `ModEngine-<version>-win64.zip`; the shim is `modengine2_launcher.exe`.
- Smithbox's `Smithbox_*.zip`; the OpenGL build is the shim because the archive has no plain `Smithbox.exe`.
- Crowbar's `Crowbar_<date>_<version>.7z`; 7-Zip unpacks the single `Crowbar.exe`.
- BSPSource's `bspsrc-jar-only.zip`; Java 24+, JDK 25 shared.
- Source 2 Viewer's `Source2Viewer.exe` and `cli-windows-x64.zip`; GUI and CLI are separate catalog entries.
- UndertaleModTool's `UndertaleModTool_v*-Windows.zip` and `UTMT_CLI_v*-Windows.zip`; GUI and CLI are separate catalog entries.
- RPFM's `rpfm-v*-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.zip`; the shim is `rpfm_ui.exe`.
- Capstone (`capstone`) in its own venv; the Python disassembly engine behind `aob-gen`.
- `pe-recon`, `aob-gen` and `ghidra-mcp-up` from `kit/`, installed as `local-script` catalog tools with generated Python shims. The encyclopedia and `context7.json` are repository content, not installed payloads.

## Left out

- `assetstudio`: the AssetStudio repository is archived. Use `assetripper`.
- `dnspy`: the original dnSpy repository is archived. Use `dnspyex`.
- `uabe`: original UABE is unsupported. Use `uabea`. UABEANext is the active source tree and publishes nightly builds, not a stable release binary; use the nightly or source build for dock support and keep stable UABEA for routine edits.
- Nintendo Switch: `nstool` v1.9.2 (2024-10-14, MIT) is the compact NSP/XCI/NCA/RomFS extractor; `switch-toolbox` uses the `Toolbox-Latest.zip` asset on the Final release (2024-01-09, GPL-3.0). Switch Toolbox's TRMDL viewing is incomplete for Scarlet/Violet; the Blender importer is the model route.
- League of Legends: `wadtools` v0.5.7 (2026-07-15, GPL-3.0) lists and extracts `.wad.client`; LTK Manager is the separate GUI mod manager and is not auto-installed.
- `umodel-ue4`: [UE Viewer / UModel](https://www.gildor.org/en/projects/umodel) supports older Unreal engines and many UE4 games. It remains a manual, target-specific route; `fmodel`, `repak` and `retoc` are this kit's modern defaults. The official page says its license is not determined, so the kit does not redistribute it or label it unsupported across UE4.
- `cpp2il-2022-binary`: Cpp2IL's latest stable binary is 2022.0.7, but [2022.1.0-pre-release.21](https://github.com/SamboyCoding/Cpp2IL/releases/tag/2022.1.0-pre-release.21) published Windows binaries on 2026-02-22. Its version prefix is not its publication year. Keep `il2cppdumper` as the automatic default; the current Cpp2IL preview is a manual alternative for targets needing its newer metadata support.
- `ue4ss-stable`: the stable UE4SS tag lags the experimental release. The kit installs `ue4ss` from `experimental-latest`.
- `bepinex6-pre2`: BepInEx 6.0.0-pre.2 is an old prerelease. Mono games get current BepInEx 5. IL2CPP gets current MelonLoader. The UnityExplorer BepInEx 6 plugin is included for a game that already has that loader.
- `cheat-engine-download`: the Cheat Engine GitHub release ships no Windows binary. The website installer bundles offers. The kit does not download it. A portable copy can be placed in `sandbox/manual/cheat-engine/` by hand.
- `reclass-rar`: ReClass.NET's only published binary is the 2019 v1.2 rar. The source kept changing through 2024 and published no newer binary. The kit can unpack a current rar. This one is stale. A portable copy can be placed in `sandbox/manual/reclass/` by hand.
- `3dmigoto-tag`: the tagged 3Dmigoto release is from 2018, the repository has continued, and that one release attaches every historical zip. A pattern cannot select a single current file. It is not part of the one-click set.
- `sinai-unityexplorer`: `sinai-dev/UnityExplorer` is archived. The kit uses the yukieiji builds.
- `frosty-rewrite`: `FrostyToolsuite/FrostyToolsuite` publishes no release. Use `frosty-editor` and `frosty-modmanager`.
- `ldtk-installer`: the old id for LDtk's installer. The installed tool is `ldtk`.
- `openbor-rar`: the old id for OpenBOR's rar. The installed tool is `openbor`.
- `fighter-factory-installer`: the old id for the dump's installer. The installed tool is `fighter-factory`.

- `de4dot`: both `de4dot/de4dot` and `ViRb3/de4dot-cex` are archived. No maintained deobfuscator binary is installed.
- `reframework`: releases are one ZIP per game, so a single asset pattern would install the wrong game's build. It stays a documented manual route.
- `codewalker`: the repository publishes no GitHub release. Use the author's build and verify its provenance.
- `cutter`: a GPL-3.0 GUI over the installed `rizin` CLI; Ghidra already provides a maintained GUI. Optional route.
- `retdec`: the last release is v5.0 (2023). Ghidra and Rizin are the maintained decompilers here.

Also not unpacked, because they install into their own directories or are not redistributable here: ReShade, NVIDIA Nsight, PIX, RTX Remix, and paid shader packs.

The Sleeping Dogs game executable and its PDB are not downloaded. TheoryEngine's README points at a file host for "Game Executable" and "Debug Symbols (PDB)". Those are the game, not a tool. The public GitHub source trees are what the kit installs.

The dump's other new folders are not a second kit. `GoDot/LimboAi` is another Godot editor build, not the LimboAI addon. IKEMEN Forge is a Claude Code plugin (`nategarelik/ikemen-forge`), not a bench executable. `UE-Modding-Tools` is a link list: the commonly used packers are already `repak`, `retoc`, FModel and UAssetGUI. ZenTools is deprecated upstream in favor of retoc. kismet-analyzer publishes no release binary. QuickBMS and the old UnrealPak rar are not unpacked.

Helldivers 2's public runtime, SDKs, ModBuilder, and loader are installed. Sleeping Dogs TheoryEngine, its SDK, and the SDK playground are installed from their public source archives. R2API is installed from the current release tag's source archive. None of those downloads include a game executable.

Duplicate trees of the same UE4SS build (`zDEV` next to the release) are not installed. One copy is enough.

## Coverage audit, 2026-10-31

Re-checked every GitHub-release entry against its current upstream release: all 41 existing patterns still matched exactly one asset. The 21 additions were selected the same way, and their archive contents were listed to confirm each shim glob: UPX 5.3.0, YARA 4.6.0, GoReSym 3.4.1, Dependencies 1.11.1, Rizin 0.9.1, System Informer 4.0.26241.138, Vineflower 1.12.0, uber-apk-signer 1.3.0, WolvenKit 9.1.0, Mod Engine 2 release-2.1.0, Smithbox 2.2.6, Crowbar 0.74, BSPSource 1.4.8, Source 2 Viewer 20.1, UndertaleModTool 0.9.2.0 and RPFM 5.3.3. ILSpy v11.2 (build 11.2.0.10050, commit c86190ef) is current; the kit keeps the self-contained zip because the plain `ILSpy_windows_*` zip needs a .NET runtime. These are dated observations, not permanent pins.

## Coverage audit, 2026-10-11 (versions refreshed 2026-10-31)

The eight app/format additions above fill gaps in the existing game-oriented catalog; capa also adds rules and signature-source dependencies. Their official latest releases/packages were checked: JADX v1.5.6 (2026-07-10), flare-capa 10.0.0 with capa/capa-rules v10.0.0 (2026-10-16), Kaitai 0.11 (2025-09-07), pyinstxtractor-ng 2026.07.03, wasm-tools v1.263.0 (2026-10-30), Apktool v3.0.3 (2026-07-20), Electron ASAR 4.3.1 on npm, and LIEF 1.0.0 on PyPI. The asset fixture records the five GitHub tools' exact binary/JAR filenames; capa rules and signatures use release source archives. These are dated observations, not permanent version pins or runtime certification.

The [coverage map](coverage.md) separates installed tools from optional/manual routes. Universal Modder provides workflows and the Starter Bundle provides agent tooling; neither makes every reverse-engineering binary present. The current Starter Bundle already includes `um`, so no second Universal Modder install is needed through this kit.

`docs/league.md`:

```md
# League of Legends and Teamfight Tactics asset route

League stores its shipped assets in `.wad.client` archives under
`Game/DATA/FINAL/`. `wadtools` is installed for read-only listing and extraction:

```text
wadtools list  -i "C:\Riot Games\League of Legends\Game\DATA\FINAL\DATA.wad.client" -F json
wadtools extract -i "<archive>.wad.client" -o "sandbox\work\league\<archive>"
```

`wadtools` scans `.bin` chunks to recover paths by default. The first listing
may download/update its shared hash tables; keep that cache outside the repo.
`DATA.wad.client` on the inspected install resolved 4,653 chunks and about
145.6 MB uncompressed, so extraction is real data work, not a metadata-only
dump.

## LTK Manager

[LTK Manager](https://github.com/LeagueToolkit/ltk-manager) is the official
LeagueToolkit mod manager and modding IDE (GPL-3.0). It is a user-facing mod
manager, not a portable extractor: its installer changes a client-side mod
setup. It is documented here but not auto-installed by this kit. Use it only
for authorised cosmetic mods you have the right to use, keep a backup, and do
not expect it to bypass Vanguard or any anti-cheat. `wadtools` is the safe
read-only route when you only need to inspect or extract assets.

## Scope

- Do not redistribute Riot assets, extracted files or mod packs.
- Do not use asset changes to gain a competitive advantage.
- Vanguard and Riot's terms still apply; a local skin mod is not permission to
  modify the online client or evade detection.
- Test against a copy and keep the original `.wad.client` files.

`docs/naruto-storm.md`:

```md
# Naruto Storm and Shinobi Striker route

## NARUTO X BORUTO Ultimate Ninja Storm Connections

`NSUNSC.exe` is a CyberConnect2 title. Its shipped data is in
`data/launch/*.cpk` (including `data1.cpk`, `data3.cpk`, `platform*.cpk`,
`sound*.cpk`) and `data/disc/movie2.cpk`; the executable also references
`data/**/*.xfbin` scripts and records. CPK is CRIWARE's archive format and
XFBIN is CyberConnect2's serialized data format.

The practical route is:

1. Back up the original `.cpk` files.
2. Extract a CPK with a CPK tool that supports the archive's encryption and
   layout. The commonly used open-source route is
   [CriPakTools](https://github.com/esperknight/CriPakTools) (list/extract/
   replace); it has no packaged release and no declared licence, so this kit
   does not redistribute it. Build it from source or use a tool you are
   licensed to run.
3. Edit `.xfbin` data with a Storm-specific toolbox such as
   [NSC-Toolbox](https://github.com/TheLeonX/NSC-Toolbox) (21 MB release;
   no declared licence, so it is manual-only here).
4. Repack or load the changed files through the game's supported mod/overlay
   route. Keep the original archive and test one change at a time.

The CPK contains copyrighted game data. Do not redistribute extracted files,
rebuilt CPKs or converted assets.

## Naruto to Boruto: Shinobi Striker

This is an Unreal Engine 4 game (`Engine/`, `NARUTO/Content/`) protected by
EasyAntiCheat. The existing Unreal tools apply to local inspection:
`fmodel`, `repak`, `retoc`, `uassetgui` and UE4SS. EAC means online play and
modded clients are a risk; use offline/cosmetic work only, never attempt to
disable or bypass EAC, and expect a modified client to be rejected.

## What is installed

`nstool` and `switch-toolbox` are for Nintendo Switch titles and do not apply
here. The Naruto CPK/XFBIN route is documented as manual because the useful
tools have no permissive redistributable release. The Unreal route uses the
already-installed Unreal tools.

```

`docs/pokemon-switch.md`:

```md
# Pokémon Switch titles on Windows: extraction, modding and native-port route

This note covers *Pokémon Sword/Shield*, *Scarlet/Violet*, *Legends: Arceus*
and *Legends: Z-A*. It is a route, not a claim that a supported one-click
converter exists. Use dumped files and keys only for games you own; do not
redistribute the ROM, extracted assets or keys.

## What engine these games use

These are Game Freak's proprietary Switch titles. They are **not Unity and not
Unreal Engine**, so AssetRipper, FModel, UABEANext and UE4SS do not convert
them. The games use NSP containers and a RomFS tree with Pokémon-specific
model/data formats such as `TRMDL`, `TRMSH`, `TRMBF`, `TRSKL`, `TRANIM`,
`BNTX`, plus game data and archive files. Confirm the actual tree for the
specific build with `nstool`; do not assume a path from another title.

## Can they be converted to a native Windows game?

There is no automatic emulator-to-native converter. A native Windows version
is a new game project that reuses extracted assets and reimplements the game
systems. For a fusion project, pick one target engine first:

| Target | Fit | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|
| **Godot 4.x** | Best default for a stylised Pokémon-scale fusion; light editor, C#, fast iteration; the kit already has Godot. | You rebuild battle, spawn, UI and save systems; third-party asset import is less mature than Unity. |
| **Unity** | Strong C# ecosystem, mature FBX/glTF importers, easier character/animation tooling. | Larger editor and project; licence/terms apply to the project and assets. |
| **Unreal Engine 5** | Only when high-fidelity rendering, large worlds or a team pipeline justify it. | Heavy editor and shader/material rebuild; usually slower to iterate for a stylised monster-battler fusion. |

For most Pokémon fusions, **Godot or Unity is more efficient than UE5**. Engine
choice does not make the port automatic: models, textures, animations, data,
battle logic and UI all need reconstruction or reimplementation.

## Extraction and conversion pipeline

1. **Inspect the container.** Keep the dump and keys outside the repository.
   Use `nstool -k <prod.keys> --fstree <game.nsp>` to list the NSP, then extract
   with `-x`. For an NCA, use the matching ticket (`--tik`) and `--fstree`
   before extracting the RomFS. Verify the title ID and build, and keep a
   read-only copy of the original dump.
2. **Textures.** `switch-toolbox` opens BNTX and many common Switch formats.
   Its TRMDL viewing is incomplete for Scarlet/Violet (upstream issue #658);
   prefer the Blender importer for models.
3. **Models and animations.** The
   [Pokémon Switch Model Importer/Exporter for Blender](https://github.com/ChicoEevee/Pokemon-Switch-Model-Importer-Blender)
   imports TRMDL/TRMSH/TRMBF and animations. It targets Blender 4.x/5.x. Its
   README says Z-A models are not working yet (checked 2026-11-01). Export to
   FBX or glTF, then import into Godot/Unity. Rebuild materials and shaders;
   do not expect the Switch material graph to survive.
4. **Game data.** `pkNX` edits Sword/Shield data; its README says Scarlet/Violet
   and Z-A are dump-only in the current build. Build it from source; it has no
   GitHub release asset. Use a copy of the dump and keep a clean backup.
5. **Saves and testing.** `PKHeX` can edit saves for test scenarios. Test asset
   swaps in the emulator with a LayeredFS `romfs` mod before rebuilding them in
   the native project.
6. **Native project.** Import only the assets you own into the chosen engine;
   build a data-driven species/move/encounter schema, then reimplement battle,
   AI, UI and save logic. A fusion of two titles means one project with a
   merged data model, not two executables glued together.

## Modding route

Ryujinx (or a compatible emulator) can load a `romfs` LayeredFS mod from the
game's mod directory. This is the fastest way to test model, texture or data
changes. It is still emulation, not a native port. Keep mods and extracted
assets out of Git unless their licences and your ownership allow distribution.

## Tool status on this kit

| Tool | Status |
|---|---|
| `nstool` | Installed, MIT, small CLI for NSP/XCI/NCA/RomFS inspection and extraction. You supply the keyset. |
| `switch-toolbox` | Installed, GPL-3.0 GUI, 44 MB. Useful for BNTX and common formats; TRMDL support is incomplete. |
| Pokémon Switch Blender importer | Manual Blender add-on; no release binary. Install the repository's add-on ZIP in Blender. |
| `pkNX` | Manual source build; GPL-3.0; Sword/Shield editing, Scarlet/Violet and Z-A dumping only. |
| `PKHeX` | Manual/upstream save editor; not an asset converter. |
| `UABEANext` | Unity-only modern UABEA fork; no stable release. Use its nightly build or source when the target is Unity, not these Pokémon titles. |
| `Kuriimu2` | Manual legacy route for supported archives; last release 1.2.1 (2021) needs .NET Core 3.1. |

## Verification

- Confirm the NSP title ID and build before extracting.
- Confirm one model imports into Blender and exports to FBX/glTF before
  converting a whole roster.
- Confirm one texture and one animation in the target engine before building a
  fusion scene.
- Record the exact game version, tool version and conversion path; a successful
  extraction is not a successful native port.

```

`docs/reverse-engineering.md`:

```md
# Reverse-engineering routes, on demand

Pick the operation, not every tool with an RE label. All supported providers use
these same CLIs; no RE MCP is registered globally by this recipe.

## Agent runs the tools

The user installs once and then asks. Do not hand the user a command list or ask them to start a server.

- Resolve the kit pointer, read `sandbox/installed.json`, and call the `sandbox/bin` shim.
- Ghidra native decompile: `analyzeHeadless`; no server is required.
- Ghidra MCP is optional. `ghidra-mcp-up --launch` waits only when a bridge is configured; otherwise use `analyzeHeadless`.
- GUI tools are launched by the agent when the task needs them. If a shim fails, read its log and report the exact error.

| Need | First choice |
| --- | --- |
| Identify an unfamiliar game, engine, loader and modding route | Installed `um scan "<verified game folder>" --format json`; Universal Modder guide |
| Disassembly/decompilation, APK/resources, assets, debugging | Existing Reverse THIS! tool shims, selected for the actual format |
| Evidence-linked static JavaScript/Electron or managed-assembly analysis | REA CLI; use its subcommand help, then retain/filter JSON locally |
| Skyrim records, Papyrus or framework syntax | Skyrim specialist skills/Forge; REA is not a replacement |

## Reuse Reverse THIS!

Resolve `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ReverseThis\kit.json`, parse its `kit_root`, and verify
that root, `sandbox/installed.json` and the selected `sandbox/bin` shim exist.
An explicit verified conversation path is a fallback. Do not search entire
drives or reinstall another Ghidra/Java/.NET/tool suite. Run the selected shim's
help or the kit's `Install-Modkit.ps1 -Doctor` before relying on it.
The folder named sandbox is **not** process isolation.

## REA CLI

The optional `rea` catalog component pins `rea-agents@6.8.0` and npm integrity;
Node ^22.23.4, ^24.22.1 or >=26.12.1 is required. If needed for the current authorized task,
install it through this standalone workbench; reuse an existing compatible shared Node runtime:

```powershell
powershell -NoProfile -File Install-Modkit.ps1 -Only rea
rea --help
rea analyze-javascript-application "<owned extracted app or ASAR>" --format json
```

Resolve the installed CLI from PATH. Other operations start with
`rea <subcommand> --help`; don't guess their arguments. Use an owned lab copy,
retain evidence/digests on disk and send only the fields the question needs to
the model. Static observations are not runtime behavior or recovered source.
Runtime capture, instrumentation and annotation are separate actions with side
effects; never run them merely to list capabilities.

Do not run `rea setup`, `rea update`, or an upstream skill installer over
bundle-managed provider trees. No extra REA skill collection is needed.
Windows native Ghidra support is experimental with specific Ghidra/JDK/format
requirements; discover the existing kit and check REA's provider doctor before
using that route. Static JavaScript analysis of an owned fixture was exercised with REA 6.8.0.

CLI-first avoids attaching a large MCP inventory to ordinary chats. A needed MCP
must be scoped and filtered using the current host's supported features. Measure
the installed version's actual schemas; old inventory sizes are not current
tool counts or billing measurements.

The official [REA 6.8.0 npm record](https://registry.npmjs.org/rea-agents/6.8.0)
was verified against the catalog integrity. Its metadata supplied no gitHead;
the upstream v6.8.0 Git ref was absent, so no invented exact-version Git link is used.

Sources: [REA upstream](https://github.com/morluto/rea),
[published 6.8.0 package](https://www.npmjs.com/package/rea-agents/v/6.8.0),
[Reverse THIS!](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/reverse-this).

```

`docs/standalone.md`:

```md
# Standalone installation and updates

Reverse THIS!, the [Starter Bundle](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/Ultimate-AI-Starter-Bundle) and [Skyrim Forge](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/SkyrimForge) have independent installers. None is a required parent installation. The bundle is useful for common compilers, AI providers and skills; Forge supplies Skyrim authoring and validation. This workbench supplies REA and analysis tools. Installing it never edits AI-provider settings, trust or credentials. The ownership matrix is in [README § Modular composition](../README.md#modular-composition); the bundle's [companion architecture](https://github.com/SenjuWoo/Ultimate-AI-Starter-Bundle/blob/main/docs/COMPANION-ARCHITECTURE.md) is the shared cross-repo contract.

Run `Install-Modkit.ps1` for the catalog or use `-Only rea`, `-Only jadx`, or another catalog ID for the current task. Normal installation resolves current owned pip, npm and NuGet packages as well as release tools. Intentional pins remain pins: REA uses 6.8.0 and its npm integrity; capa uses a matched package/rules/signatures release. A numerically newer valid owned tool is retained. Unknown release labels require their recorded identity rather than an invented ordering.

## Overlap and synchronization

Overlap is allowed only when one owner is clear. A consumer detects a shared tool or runtime first, compares its version and payload health, then reuses a current copy, updates an older owned copy in place, or preserves a newer or unknown copy and reports it. It does not silently reinstall or create a parallel copy.

- Kit-owned tools use `.kit-ok`, `.kit-record.json` and payload hashes. A current marker is skipped; an older one is updated; `-NoUpdate` keeps valid installed bytes; `-Force` is the explicit reinstall.
- Shared runtimes (`UABS_PYTHON`, Node, Java, .NET) are detected before installation. A missing prerequisite is installed once; a working existing one is reused rather than duplicated, and an explicitly bound incompatible interpreter is reported instead of replaced.
- External tools require `-ToolDirectories` or `UABS_TOOL_ROOTS` with a valid owner receipt; there is no drive scan. Unknown or unverified copies are preserved and reported.
- 7-Zip and DirectXTex are this kit's payloads. Forge may reference `sandbox/bin/7z.cmd` and the DirectXTex tools instead of installing a second copy.

The registered `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ReverseThis\kit.json` identifies the one canonical owner kit. Another source checkout uses that owner's sandbox and shims, with the new checkout's catalog/installer. It does not silently move the pointer or install another tool suite. An invalid pointer fails with its cause. `-InstallRoot "<absolute isolated root>"` deliberately isolates tool state and never writes the shared pointer; use this for tests. The installer log is `<owner>/sandbox/log/modkit.log`; `-DebugLog` includes package/archive details.

Shared Python resolves `UABS_PYTHON` in Process, then User scope, then validated base interpreters on PATH and the official manager's documented user locations. Virtual environments and AI-app runtimes are excluded. Existing manager-owned Python receives a patch update on its current feature line, never a feature-version replacement. Missing compatible Python uses the official `Python.PythonInstallManager` winget package, then `pymanager install --yes default`. Dependencies remain in tool venvs. An explicitly bound incompatible interpreter is reported rather than silently replaced.

Node and npm must be a compatible pair in the same shared installation. ASAR requires Node 22.12+; REA 6.8.0 requires ^22.23.4, ^24.22.1 or >=26.12.1. Generated npm shims bind the selected Node directory even if the caller's PATH starts with another Node. Existing winget-owned Node LTS at the standard installation path receives its targeted `OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS` update. Missing Node uses that official package. A working incompatible installation whose owner cannot be established is preserved and reported; a parallel runtime is not installed. Java and .NET retain the existing official winget prerequisite routes.

`-NoUpdate` retains valid installed packages and skips shared-runtime patch updates; missing selected tools can still install. `-Force` explicitly reinstalls and cannot be combined with `-NoUpdate`. `-Plan` prints the catalog without network or writes. `-Doctor` and `-Offline` inspect owner receipts/files without network, runtime setup or writes. Doctor does not prove target compatibility. Normal updates retain payload/state backups and roll back failed replacements.

For an already owned tool outside the canonical sandbox, supply its directory with `-ToolDirectories "<directory>"`. A 1.4 owner `.kit-record.json` must identify the catalog ID/version and bind shim/package metadata to file hashes. The installer validates it without running a GUI, then reuses and updates that same directory with retained backups. Explicit `UABS_TOOL_ROOTS` candidates are checked only at the root, `<root>/<catalog-id>` and `<root>/sandbox/tools/<catalog-id>`; there is no drive scan. Unverified legacy receipts are reported and preserved. Receipt hashes cover the named launchers/package metadata or one source representative; they are not a signature of every payload byte.

A compatible same-major REA already on PATH is reused as unowned with its observed version and launcher hash. Unknown, older or incompatible unowned REA is preserved and reported for its owner's update/verification, rather than duplicated. `-Force` is the explicit choice to install an isolated owned copy. Automatic in-place updates apply only where ownership is known; an arbitrary external file never grants update authority.

For a released ZIP installation, run `Update-ReverseThis.ps1`. It selects the official current source ZIP, requires its GitHub SHA-256 asset digest, validates the extracted release receipt and updates only public source paths. It verifies the previous `RELEASE.json` before replacement, refuses locally changed public files or new-path collisions, and retains prior source files under `sandbox/backups/source-*`. Private sandbox work/tools/state stay in place. Git development roots and linked write destinations are refused; review and fast-forward those through Git instead.

`-Destination <absolute directory>` (alias `-KitRoot`) selects an existing released kit or a missing/empty fresh source destination. A nonempty directory without a valid existing release is preserved. `-Archive <zip> -Sha256 <expected digest>` handles an independently verified local artifact, including bootstrapping 1.3 with the updater extracted from a verified 1.4 archive. `-Plan`, `-NoUpdate` and `-Offline` disable source updates without downloads or writes. Logs are `sandbox/log/source-update.log`; `-DebugLog` adds the selected release/commit/path details. Invalid fresh archives fail before creating the destination. A failed fresh deployment can leave its diagnostic sandbox; preserve those logs and select a clean destination for retry.

Run the deployed `Install-Modkit.ps1` after source deployment. A first normal run without `-InstallRoot` registers the new canonical owner. To refresh only an existing kit's installed choices, pass its `installed.json` tool IDs through `-Only id1,id2`; omitting `-Only` selects the full current catalog. `-InstallRoot` isolates tool state and never deploys source or registers a canonical owner. No updater or wrapper should guess catalog contents or rewrite the installed choices.

```

`encyclopedia/CLAIMS.md`:

```md
# Capability Claims: Dated Observations, Not Permanent Laws

Every limit in this encyclopedia is an observation about a named tool, version and mechanism on a date. It is not a statement about what is possible forever. A later runtime, loader, decompiler or technique may remove it. When that happens, update the card and the test; do not defend the old sentence.

## Write a limit like this

- Name the mechanism: "a ReShade effect", "a UE4 cooked package", "this installer".
- Name the version and date: "as of ReShade 6.x (2026-10)".
- Name the escape hatch: "a ReShade add-on can issue draws", "runtime introspection may recover names".
- Name the re-test trigger: "re-check when the runtime exposes writable depth".

Bad: "ReShade cannot write depth."

Good: "As of the ReShade 6.x effect runtime (2026-10), the game depth texture is exposed read-only; an add-on or a draw hook is the current route to geometry. Re-test after a runtime change."

## Current scoped limits

| Limit | Scope | Escape hatch and re-test |
|---|---|---|
| ReShade effect depth input is read-only | ReShade effect runtime, 6.x (2026-10) | A ReShade add-on (C++ API) can issue draws; a D3D11 draw/depth hook is the engine-level route. Re-test if effects gain writable depth. |
| A ReShade effect renders a fullscreen pass, not a mesh | ReShade effect runtime | Use an add-on, a proxy DLL or an engine hook for geometry. Re-test after an effect-runtime API change. |
| UE3/UE4 cooked packages strip source names | Cooked UE builds | Runtime object-name tables, SDK dumps, or a reimplementation. Re-test with a newer cooker or a title that ships symbols. |
| Native decompilers output approximations | Named decompiler and version | Use a debugger or tracer as ground truth; a newer decompiler may improve the result. |
| The catalog installs one verified asset per tool | This installer's current catalog | Place any other tool in `sandbox/manual/<name>/`; update the catalog pattern when upstream changes. |
| One live kit per user | This installer's ownership model | Use `-InstallRoot` for isolated kits; the pointer is a safety rule, not a capability limit. |
| Anti-cheat tolerance is game- and patch-specific | The game's current official policy, not a tool capability | Check the policy and record its date; cosmetic-only, no competitive advantage, user accepts the account risk. Re-test after a patch. See `anticheat-risk.md`. |

## What is not a capability limit

Validation gates, backups, license/provenance rules, and anti-cheat risk decisions stay. They exist to avoid data loss and account loss, not to declare what a tool can do. Keep them separate from capability statements. Anti-cheat is a dated policy assessment, not a permanent "no": see `anticheat-risk.md`.

## Retiring a limit

1. Reproduce the old limit on the current version.
2. If it still holds, add the version and date to the card.
3. If it is gone, update the card, the error registry and the test; keep a one-line history in `CHANGELOG.md`.
4. If a new mechanism only partly removes it, record both: what still holds and what changed.

```

    },
    {

      "signature": "Interceptor.attach succeeds but onEnter never fires; the game behaves normally",
      "cause": "The hooked wrapper/constructor is not on the executed path, or the game uses a different module/thread/copy of the object.",
      "fix": "Apply the Downstream Sink Protocol: attach a counter and backtrace at Present()/EndScene/UI sink, walk the stack to the real producer, and hook that leaf function. Confirm the module base and RVA.",
      "verify": "The sink counter advances and the leaf hook logs the expected arguments; disabling the hook restores the original behavior."
    },
    {
      "id": "passthrough-geometry-swimming",
      "signature": "Guest geometry slides or swims against the host camera; near objects lag behind far objects",
      "cause": "The guest pose is applied without the camera matrix, or the host/guest depth conventions or units differ.",
      "fix": "Embed the guest view-projection and camera world matrix in the shared-memory header; reproject with the inverse guest VP and the host VP; convert units once; add the one-frame pose-lag term. Validate with fake_host_d3d11.exe.",
      "verify": "A static guest landmark stays on the same host pixel during a pure camera rotation; a translation test keeps the floor contact point stable."
    },

      "id": "defender-single-file-installer",
      "signature": "Windows Defender flags a PyInstaller one-file mod installer as Trojan:Win32/Wacatac or similar",
      "cause": "One-file self-extractors unpack to a temp directory and execute, a pattern that matches generic heuristics; the signature is usually a false positive.",
      "fix": "Ship a one-folder build (PyInstaller --onedir) instead of --onefile, sign the executable if possible, publish SHA256SUMS, and document the expected hash. Do not tell users to disable Defender.",
      "verify": "The folder build scans clean on VirusTotal (or the vendor confirms the false positive); the published hash matches the downloaded file."
    },
    {
      "id": "reshade-depth-inverted",
      "signature": "ReShade depth effects are inverted, sky is near, or AO/DOF works inside-out",
      "cause": "The host uses a reversed-Z depth buffer and the shader assumes standard 0..1 depth.",
      "fix": "Set the depth-reversed flag (GTA V, UE4/5, many D3D11/12 titles) and linearize with near*far/(near + raw*(far-near)); verify with a readback.",
      "verify": "A near object reads a smaller linear depth than a far object; fake_host_d3d11.exe passes both draw orders."
    },
    {
      "id": "ipc-torn-frame",
      "signature": "Pose or depth values jump, matrices contain impossible values, or the reader sees half an update",
      "cause": "The reader copied the payload while the writer was updating it, or the sequence counter was not checked.",
      "fix": "Use the seqlock protocol: writer stores odd seq, payload, even seq; reader retries when seq is odd or changed. Keep the payload trivially copyable and the sequence 32-bit atomic.",
      "verify": "ipc_link_test.exe passes 50,000 frames with no torn read; a deliberate writer pause still yields a clean previous frame."
    },
    {
      "id": "proxy-double-load",
      "signature": "Two overlays fight for dxgi.dll/d3d11.dll, or the game loads the proxy twice and hooks fire twice",
      "cause": "Two tools claim the same proxy slot, or the loader is copied into both the game root and a subdirectory.",
      "fix": "Keep one owner per proxy name; check loaded modules before injecting; use the loader's documented proxy name and remove duplicate copies.",
      "verify": "The module list shows one instance of the proxy; the hook counter advances once per frame."
    }
  ]
}

`encyclopedia/README.md`:

```md
# AI Game Modding & Reverse Engineering Encyclopedia

This directory is the Context7-indexed knowledge base for the Reverse THIS! workbench. It is written for an agent that has to make a decision and run a command, not for a reader who wants an overview.

## Start here

| Need | File |
|---|---|
| Choose the cheapest viable modding route | [ROUTING.md](ROUTING.md) |
| Identify an engine and its coordinate/depth conventions | [engines/](engines/) |
| Copy a drop-in IPC, shader or proxy bridge | [blueprints/](blueprints/) |
| Operate Ghidra MCP, Frida or AOB signatures | [re-recipes/](re-recipes/) |
| Record a target's pinned build | [games/TEMPLATE.md](games/TEMPLATE.md) |
| Map an error signature to a cause | [ERROR-REGISTRY.json](ERROR-REGISTRY.json) |
| See where the Gemini draft was wrong | [VALIDATION.md](VALIDATION.md) |
| Scope a limit to a tool, version and escape hatch | [CLAIMS.md](CLAIMS.md) |
| Decide whether an online mod is tolerated | [anticheat-risk.md](anticheat-risk.md) |

## Non-negotiables

- A successful install is not a successful hook. Prove it with a downstream sink.
- A decompiled function is a hypothesis until it runs under a debugger or tracer.
- A pattern match is a candidate until it resolves in two different builds.
- In the ReShade effect runtime (6.x, 2026-10), the game depth texture is read-only; an add-on or a draw hook is the current route to geometry. Treat that as a versioned limit, not a law.
- Anti-cheat is a risk tier, not a blanket ban: some games tolerate cosmetic client-side mods, others flag the injection method itself. Check the current policy, keep online changes cosmetic and reversible, and do not ship a competitive-advantage change. See [anticheat-risk.md](anticheat-risk.md).
- A limit is a dated observation: name the mechanism, version and escape hatch, and re-test when either changes. See [CLAIMS.md](CLAIMS.md).

## Layout

```
encyclopedia/
  ROUTING.md                 6-tier escalation ladder and stop rules
  CLAIMS.md                  how limits are scoped and retired
  anticheat-risk.md          risk tiers and dated policy evidence
  engines/                   engine fingerprints, axes, units, depth, proxy DLLs
  blueprints/                C++/HLSL/Python templates and offline harnesses
  re-recipes/                Ghidra, Frida and AOB playbooks
  games/                     version-pinned field notes
  ERROR-REGISTRY.json        error signature -> cause -> fix
```

Context7 reads this directory because `context7.json` at the repository root sets `"folders": ["encyclopedia"]`. Root markdown is always indexed by Context7; the runtime directories (`sandbox`, `bin`, `.git`, `tests`, `node_modules`, `**/temp`) are excluded.
