`encyclopedia/ROUTING.md`:

```md
# 6-Tier Modding Route Escalation Ladder

Pick the lowest tier that can produce the required behavior. A higher tier is not more impressive; it is more expensive, more fragile and harder to verify. Record the tier in the target's field note.

| Tier | Route | Use when | Cost | Minimum proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Data overrides (loose files, plugin records, INI/CVAR, pak patch) | The engine already loads the asset or value from a replaceable source | Minutes; no code | Changed value appears in game and survives a restart |
| 2 | Loader APIs (SKSE, BepInEx, MelonLoader, UE4SS, ScriptHookV, ASI) | A supported script/plugin surface exists for the behavior | Hours; version-locked to the loader | Loader log shows the plugin loaded; downstream sink fires |
| 3 | Managed patching (Harmony prefix/postfix/transpiler, Mono.Cecil, IL2CPP detour) | The behavior lives in managed code or a stable managed entry point | Hours to days; update-sensitive | Patched method runs; original behavior preserved when disabled |
| 4 | Native proxy DLL / IAT hook / inline detour | No supported surface, or the behavior is in native code | Days; crash-prone | Hook fires under a debugger; unhook restores original bytes |
| 5 | Headless simulation / decompiler library (Ghidra headless, Unicorn, custom reimplementation) | You need to understand or replay the system without the game | Days; may not be runtime-compatible | Reimplementation matches recorded input/output traces |
| 6 | Concurrent shared-memory passthrough | Two runtimes must coexist frame-by-frame and no in-engine route exists | Weeks; timing and lifetime bugs | Offline IPC harness passes; live run shows aligned frames with no torn reads |

## Stop rules

1. Do not climb a tier because it is familiar. Climb only when the lower tier does not express the behavior (yet).
2. Tier 3 and below may be tested in a live single-player session with saves backed up.
3. Tier 4-6 require the offline harness first: `blueprints/fake_host_d3d11.cpp` for depth/pixel math and `blueprints/ipc_link_test.cpp` for shared memory.
4. Anti-cheat is a risk tier, not a blanket ban: check the current policy, keep online changes cosmetic and reversible, and do not ship a competitive-advantage change. Tier 4-6 hooks in a live anti-cheat session are high risk; validate offline first. See [anticheat-risk.md](anticheat-risk.md).
5. If a higher tier does not name its downstream sink, it is not ready.

## Retiring a limit

A limit in this ladder is current, not eternal. If a higher tier now solves what a lower tier could not, or a runtime change removes a blocker, record the tool version and date, update the tier row, and add a line to `CHANGELOG.md`. See [CLAIMS.md](CLAIMS.md) for the wording rule.

## Downstream Sink Protocol

A hook that installs without crashing is not proof. Trace forward from the hook to an observable sink:

1. **Choose the sink before writing the hook.** `IDXGISwapChain::Present`, `EndScene`, a UI text draw, a frame counter written to a log, or a game event with a visible result.
2. **Attach a counter at the sink.** Log frame number, hook invocation count and a payload hash.
3. **If the hook is silent, walk backward.** Set a breakpoint or Frida `Thread.backtrace()` at the sink and walk the stack until you find the producer that should have called your hook.
4. **If the sink never fires, the hook is on the wrong path.** Wrapper/constructor hooks commonly miss the real call site; hook the leaf function the sink actually uses.
5. **Record the evidence** (log excerpt, frame numbers, hash) in the field note. "It works" is not evidence.

## Coordinate conversion checklist

Before writing a conversion, record all six values:

- handedness (left/right)
- up axis (+Y or +Z)
- forward axis and sign
- unit scale to meters
- rotation convention (degrees/radians, yaw origin, sign)
- parent/child transform order

Then write the matrix and test one known landmark in both runtimes. A conversion that looks right but mirrors one axis will pass a single-point test and fail a traversal.

```

`encyclopedia/VALIDATION.md`:

```md
# Validation of the Gemini draft

The draft was technically useful but wrong or unsafe in the places below. The implementation in this directory follows the corrected column.

| # | Draft claim | Verdict | Correction and evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `context7.json` with `projectTitle`, `description`, `folders`, `excludeFolders`, `excludeFiles`, `rules` | Correct | The schema also supports `$schema` and `previousVersions`; `excludeFiles` takes filenames only. Confirmed at <https://context7.com/docs/library-owners>. `$schema` was added. |
| 2 | Creation Engine card covers `.bsa`/`.ba2` | Wrong | Skyrim LE/SE use `.bsa`; Fallout 4 and Starfield use `.ba2` (Creation Engine 2). Do not present them as interchangeable. |
| 3 | Strip `FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING` from `CreateFileW` | Unsafe | Unbuffered I/O requires sector-aligned buffers and offsets; stripping the flag changes cache behavior and can break the loader. Hook only for path redirection, preserve flags, and mirror them in any replacement handle. |
| 4 | Ghidra MCP over Streamable HTTP at `http://127.0.0.1:8080/mcp` | Wrong port | GhidraMCP's Ghidra-side HTTP server listens on `127.0.0.1:8080`; the MCP bridge is a separate process (default `127.0.0.1:8081`, transport `streamable-http`). Probe both. See <https://github.com/LaurieWired/GhidraMCP>. |
| 5 | ReShade shader performs hardware depth testing and 6-DoF depth reprojection | Partly wrong | As of the ReShade 6.x effect runtime (2026-10), the game depth texture is read-only: an effect can read it and reproject color, but cannot write it or inject geometry. A ReShade **add-on** (C++ API) can issue draw calls; a D3D11 draw/depth hook is the engine-level route. Re-test after a runtime change. See the ReShade FX reference (fullscreen-triangle example) and the add-on event list. |
| 6 | `pefile` and `capstone` for AOB generation | Half right | `capstone` is required to decode instruction shapes. `pefile` is not: the helper maps RVA to file offset from the PE section table with the standard library, so only `capstone` is installed. |
| 7 | BepInEx 6 as a general loader | Imprecise | BepInEx 6 is still a pre-release line. Mono targets use BepInEx 5; IL2CPP targets use MelonLoader (or a pinned BepInEx 6 build when the target requires it). |
| 8 | GTA V card: `-nobattleye` and ScriptHookV | Correct with a limit | `-nobattleye` and ScriptHookV are single-player only; the Enhanced edition is `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` and ships BattlEye. This is not an online route. |
| 9 | GTA V reversed-Z | Correct | ReShade's GTA V depth fix and community depth guides confirm the reversed buffer; the card still requires a readback before shipping. |
| 10 | UE "1.0 = 1cm" | Correct for UE4/5 | UE4/5 default to 1 Unreal Unit = 1 cm and Z-up, left-handed. UE3 titles frequently rescale; verify per game. |
| 11 | Skyrim units | Missing | Creation Engine uses 64 units per yard: 1 unit = 0.5625 in = 1.42875 cm; 128 units ≈ 1.8288 m. Added to the card. |
| 12 | Field notes with build IDs but no hashes | Incomplete | The template requires EXE SHA256 and loader version. Unknown values are marked `not recorded`; no hash was invented. |

## What was kept

The 6-tier ladder, downstream-sink discipline, offline-harness gate, seqlock header, proxy-entity bridge, AOB masking approach and the error registry are sound. They are implemented as written, with the corrections above.

```

`encyclopedia/anticheat-risk.md`:

```md
# Anti-cheat: Risk Tiers, Not a Blanket Ban

Anti-cheat is not one rule. Some multiplayer games tolerate cosmetic client-side mods; some ban any client modification; some act only on competitive advantage, economy changes or DLC unlocks. The policy changes by game and by patch, so the only safe statement is: read the current official policy, name the method, and record the account risk.

This page is a decision record, not an evasion guide. It does not describe how to defeat a detector. It describes how to decide whether a mod belongs online at all.

## Dated evidence

- **Marvel Rivals, 2026-10-15** — the official post says the team wants to preserve customization "as long as the fairness of the game is not compromised", but mods that use **memory injection and modification** "instantly trigger" anti-cheat. Prohibited examples: structural model/texture changes that create asymmetric line-of-sight, recoil/VFX removal, audio changes, and enemy model enlargement. It names `pystubber.asi` and `MarvelRivalsUnmountBlocker.asi` as files that trigger detection. Suspensions were lifted, but the post says that is not permission. <https://www.marvelrivals.com/20261015/41525_1316797.html>
- **Monster Hunter Wilds, official warning (Ver. 1.040)** — Capcom reports unauthorized modification of investigation/Field Survey data, filters or blocks modified quests, and may suspend accounts for fraudulent activity. The warning targets modified quest data, not cosmetic skins, but it is not a blanket permission for client modification. <https://www.monsterhunter.com/wilds/en-us/topics/unauthorized-modification-data/>

Both policies can change with a patch. Record the URL and date in the target's field note.

## Risk tiers

| Tier | Route | Typical risk | Minimum check |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Official mod support, curated workshop, official asset tools | Lowest | The game's own terms and the tool's supported version |
| 2 | Cosmetic client-side asset swap, no gameplay effect, no memory injection | Tolerated by some games; account risk remains | Current policy names the method or is silent; mod is reversible |
| 3 | Cosmetic result delivered by injection, ASI, DLL proxy or memory patch | May trigger anti-cheat even when the visual result is harmless | Policy does not name the method; accept that detection is possible |
| 4 | Competitive advantage, economy change, DLC unlock, hitbox/visibility/audio/recoil change | Prohibited in practice | Do not ship |

Tier 2 is the only tier where "tolerated" is a reasonable word, and even there it is a policy observation with a date, not a guarantee.

## Decision checklist

1. Link the current official policy and record its date.
2. Does the policy name the delivery method (ASI, injection, pak, loose file)?
3. Does the mod change hitboxes, visibility, recoil, audio, model scale, drop rates or ownership? If yes, it is Tier 4.
4. Is there an official loader or asset path? Prefer it over injection.
5. Can the user revert it cleanly, with saves and original files backed up?
6. Has the user accepted the account risk in writing in the field note? Never assume it.

## Detection surfaces (high level)

Anti-cheat may check file integrity, loaded modules and signatures, memory regions, server-side state and player reports. A cosmetic mod can still be flagged if its delivery method resembles a cheat loader or if another player reports it. The reliable way to reduce risk is to avoid the behaviors the policy names, use supported asset paths, and keep the change cosmetic; there is no guaranteed undetected method, and this project does not claim one. Community reports of a cosmetic mod running without a ban are anecdotal and patch-specific; they do not establish a safe method.

## If a policy changes

Update this page with the new URL and date, update the target's field note, and add a `CHANGELOG.md` line. If a previously tolerated method becomes prohibited, move it to Tier 4 rather than arguing with the new text.

```

`encyclopedia/blueprints/fake_host_d3d11.cpp`:

```cpp
// fake_host_d3d11.cpp — offline WARP harness for reversed-Z depth semantics.
//
// Build (MinGW-w64):
//   g++ -std=c++17 -O2 fake_host_d3d11.cpp -o fake_host_d3d11.exe -ld3d11 -ld3dcompiler
// Run:
//   .\fake_host_d3d11.exe
//
// It creates a WARP D3D11 device, renders a full-screen triangle at two depths
// with D3D11_COMPARISON_GREATER_EQUAL, reads the pixel back, and proves the
// nearer draw wins in both submission orders. This is the 2-second check for
// "is my host depth convention reversed-Z?" without launching a game.
#include <d3d11.h>
#include <d3dcompiler.h>

#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <cstring>

template <class T>
struct Com {
    T* p = nullptr;
    ~Com() { if (p) p->Release(); }
    T** operator&() { return &p; }
    T* operator->() const { return p; }
    explicit operator bool() const { return p != nullptr; }
    void reset() { if (p) { p->Release(); p = nullptr; } }
};

static const char* kShader = R"(
cbuffer Params : register(b0) { float g_depth; float3 g_color; };
struct VSOut { float4 pos : SV_Position; };
VSOut VSMain(uint id : SV_VertexID) {
    float2 uv = float2((id << 1) & 2, id & 2);
    VSOut o;
    o.pos = float4(uv * float2(2.0, -2.0) + float2(-1.0, 1.0), g_depth, 1.0);
    return o;
}
float4 PSMain(VSOut i) : SV_Target { return float4(g_color, 1.0); }
)";

struct Params {
    float depth;
    float color[3];
};

static bool ok(HRESULT hr, const char* what) {
    if (FAILED(hr)) {
        std::printf("FAIL %s hr=0x%08lX\n", what, static_cast<unsigned long>(hr));
        return false;
    }
    return true;
}

static bool compile(const char* entry, const char* target, Com<ID3DBlob>& blob) {
    Com<ID3DBlob> error;
    HRESULT hr = D3DCompile(kShader, std::strlen(kShader), nullptr, nullptr, nullptr, entry, target,
                            D3DCOMPILE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL1, 0, &blob, &error);
    if (FAILED(hr)) {
        std::printf("FAIL compile %s: %s\n", entry,
                    error ? static_cast<const char*>(error->GetBufferPointer()) : "unknown");
        return false;
    }
    return true;
}

static float read_red(ID3D11DeviceContext* ctx, ID3D11Texture2D* target, ID3D11Texture2D* staging) {
    ctx->CopyResource(staging, target);
    D3D11_MAPPED_SUBRESOURCE map{};
    if (FAILED(ctx->Map(staging, 0, D3D11_MAP_READ, 0, &map))) return -1.0f;
    const float* pixel = static_cast<const float*>(map.pData);
    const float red = pixel[0];
    ctx->Unmap(staging, 0);
    return red;
}

int main() {
    Com<ID3D11Device> device;
    Com<ID3D11DeviceContext> ctx;
    D3D_FEATURE_LEVEL level{};
    HRESULT hr = D3D11CreateDevice(nullptr, D3D_DRIVER_TYPE_WARP, nullptr, 0,
                                   nullptr, 0, D3D11_SDK_VERSION, &device, &level, &ctx);
    if (FAILED(hr)) {
        std::printf("FAIL D3D11CreateDevice WARP hr=0x%08lX\n", static_cast<unsigned long>(hr));
        return 1;
    }
    std::printf("WARP device created, feature level 0x%X\n", level);
    Com<ID3DBlob> vsBlob, psBlob;
    if (!compile("VSMain", "vs_5_0", vsBlob) || !compile("PSMain", "ps_5_0", psBlob)) return 1;
    Com<ID3D11VertexShader> vs;
    Com<ID3D11PixelShader> ps;
    if (FAILED(device->CreateVertexShader(vsBlob->GetBufferPointer(), vsBlob->GetBufferSize(), nullptr, &vs)) ||
        FAILED(device->CreatePixelShader(psBlob->GetBufferPointer(), psBlob->GetBufferSize(), nullptr, &ps))) {
        std::printf("FAIL create shaders\n");
        return 1;
    }

    D3D11_TEXTURE2D_DESC colorDesc{};
    colorDesc.Width = 4;
    colorDesc.Height = 4;
    colorDesc.MipLevels = 1;
    colorDesc.ArraySize = 1;
    colorDesc.Format = DXGI_FORMAT_R32G32B32A32_FLOAT;
    colorDesc.SampleDesc.Count = 1;
    colorDesc.Usage = D3D11_USAGE_DEFAULT;
    colorDesc.BindFlags = D3D11_BIND_RENDER_TARGET;
    Com<ID3D11Texture2D> color;
    if (!ok(device->CreateTexture2D(&colorDesc, nullptr, &color), "color texture")) return 1;
    Com<ID3D11RenderTargetView> rtv;
    if (!ok(device->CreateRenderTargetView(color.p, nullptr, &rtv), "rtv")) return 1;

    D3D11_TEXTURE2D_DESC depthDesc = colorDesc;
    depthDesc.Format = DXGI_FORMAT_D32_FLOAT;
    depthDesc.BindFlags = D3D11_BIND_DEPTH_STENCIL;
    Com<ID3D11Texture2D> depth;
    if (!ok(device->CreateTexture2D(&depthDesc, nullptr, &depth), "depth texture")) return 1;
    Com<ID3D11DepthStencilView> dsv;
    if (!ok(device->CreateDepthStencilView(depth.p, nullptr, &dsv), "dsv")) return 1;

    D3D11_TEXTURE2D_DESC stagingDesc = colorDesc;
    stagingDesc.Usage = D3D11_USAGE_STAGING;
    stagingDesc.BindFlags = 0;
    stagingDesc.CPUAccessFlags = D3D11_CPU_ACCESS_READ;
    Com<ID3D11Texture2D> staging;
    if (!ok(device->CreateTexture2D(&stagingDesc, nullptr, &staging), "staging texture")) return 1;

    D3D11_DEPTH_STENCIL_DESC dsDesc{};
    dsDesc.DepthEnable = TRUE;
    dsDesc.DepthWriteMask = D3D11_DEPTH_WRITE_MASK_ALL;
    dsDesc.DepthFunc = D3D11_COMPARISON_GREATER_EQUAL;  // reversed-Z
    Com<ID3D11DepthStencilState> dsState;
    if (!ok(device->CreateDepthStencilState(&dsDesc, &dsState), "depth state")) return 1;

    D3D11_BUFFER_DESC cbDesc{};
    cbDesc.ByteWidth = sizeof(Params);
    cbDesc.Usage = D3D11_USAGE_DYNAMIC;
    cbDesc.BindFlags = D3D11_BIND_CONSTANT_BUFFER;
    cbDesc.CPUAccessFlags = D3D11_CPU_ACCESS_WRITE;
    Com<ID3D11Buffer> cb;
    if (!ok(device->CreateBuffer(&cbDesc, nullptr, &cb), "constant buffer")) return 1;

    auto draw = [&](float z, float r, float g, float b) {
        D3D11_MAPPED_SUBRESOURCE map{};
        if (FAILED(ctx->Map(cb.p, 0, D3D11_MAP_WRITE_DISCARD, 0, &map))) return false;
        auto* p = static_cast<Params*>(map.pData);
        p->depth = z;
        p->color[0] = r;
        p->color[1] = g;
        p->color[2] = b;
        ctx->Unmap(cb.p, 0);
        ctx->Draw(3, 0);
        return true;
    };

    const float clearColor[4] = {0, 0, 0, 1};
    const float clearDepth = 0.0f;  // reversed-Z far plane
    ctx->ClearRenderTargetView(rtv.p, clearColor);
    ctx->ClearDepthStencilView(dsv.p, D3D11_CLEAR_DEPTH, clearDepth, 0);
    ctx->OMSetRenderTargets(1, &rtv.p, dsv.p);
    ctx->OMSetDepthStencilState(dsState.p, 0);
    D3D11_VIEWPORT vp{0, 0, 4, 4, 0, 1};
    ctx->RSSetViewports(1, &vp);
    ctx->IASetPrimitiveTopology(D3D11_PRIMITIVE_TOPOLOGY_TRIANGLELIST);
    ctx->VSSetShader(vs.p, nullptr, 0);
    ctx->PSSetShader(ps.p, nullptr, 0);
    ctx->VSSetConstantBuffers(0, 1, &cb.p);
    ctx->PSSetConstantBuffers(0, 1, &cb.p);

    // Near (0.9) then far (0.1): the far draw must fail the depth test.
    if (!draw(0.9f, 1, 0, 0) || !draw(0.1f, 0, 0, 1)) return 1;
    float red = read_red(ctx.p, color.p, staging.p);
    if (!(red > 0.9f)) {
        std::printf("FAIL near-then-far: red=%.3f (expected the near draw to win)\n", red);
        return 1;
    }
    std::printf("PASS near-then-far red=%.3f\n", red);

    // Far (0.1) then near (0.9): the near draw must overwrite.
    ctx->ClearRenderTargetView(rtv.p, clearColor);
    ctx->ClearDepthStencilView(dsv.p, D3D11_CLEAR_DEPTH, clearDepth, 0);
    if (!draw(0.1f, 0, 0, 1) || !draw(0.9f, 1, 0, 0)) return 1;
    red = read_red(ctx.p, color.p, staging.p);
    if (!(red > 0.9f)) {
        std::printf("FAIL far-then-near: red=%.3f (expected the near draw to win)\n", red);
        return 1;
    }
    std::printf("PASS far-then-near red=%.3f\n", red);
    std::printf("PASS reversed-Z GREATER_EQUAL semantics\n");
    return 0;
}

```

`encyclopedia/blueprints/ipc_link_test.cpp`:

```cpp
// ipc_link_test.cpp — offline harness for ipc_seqlock.h.
//
// Build (MinGW-w64): g++ -std=c++17 -O2 -pthread ipc_link_test.cpp -o ipc_link_test.exe
// Run: .\ipc_link_test.exe
//
// It creates a Local\ mapping, writes 50,000 frames from one thread while a
// reader pulls the newest clean snapshot, and fails on a torn read, a missing
// frame, or a non-monotonic frame id. No game, no D3D device, no admin rights.
#include "ipc_seqlock.h"

#include <atomic>
#include <chrono>
#include <cstdio>
#include <string>
#include <thread>

using namespace reverse_this;

int main() {
    const std::wstring name = L"Local\\ReverseThisIpcTest_" + std::to_wstring(GetCurrentProcessId());
    bool existed = false;
    auto buffer = SharedFrameBuffer::Create(name, &existed);
    if (!buffer) {
        std::printf("FAIL create mapping (GetLastError=%lu)\n", GetLastError());
        return 1;
    }
    std::printf("mapping %ls slots=%u%s\n", name.c_str(), buffer->slot_count(),
                existed ? " (reused)" : "");

    constexpr uint64_t kFrames = 50000;
    std::atomic<bool> done{false};
    std::atomic<uint64_t> written{0};
    std::atomic<uint64_t> torn{0};
    std::atomic<uint64_t> reads{0};

    std::thread writer([&] {
        for (uint64_t id = 1; id <= kFrames; ++id) {
            Frame frame{};
            frame.frame_id = id;
            frame.rgba = static_cast<uint32_t>(id);
            frame.linear_depth = static_cast<float>(id) * 0.5f;
            frame.near_z = 0.1f;
            frame.far_z = 1000.0f;
            for (int i = 0; i < 16; ++i) {
                frame.view_proj[i] = static_cast<float>(id + i);
                frame.camera_world[i] = static_cast<float>(id - i);
            }
            frame.timestamp_ns = static_cast<uint64_t>(
                std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::nanoseconds>(
                    std::chrono::steady_clock::now().time_since_epoch())
                    .count());
            if (!buffer->Write(frame)) {
                std::printf("FAIL write %llu\n", static_cast<unsigned long long>(id));
                std::exit(1);
            }
            written.store(id, std::memory_order_release);
        }
        done.store(true, std::memory_order_release);
    });

    uint64_t last = 0;
    uint64_t accepted = 0;
    while (!done.load(std::memory_order_acquire) || last < kFrames) {
        Frame frame{};
        if (!buffer->ReadLatest(&frame)) {
            torn.fetch_add(1);
            if (torn.load() > 1000000) {
                std::printf("FAIL reader could not get a clean frame\n");
                std::exit(1);
            }
            continue;
        }
        reads.fetch_add(1);
        // A clean but stale slot is normal when newer slots are mid-write; the
        // seqlock guarantees consistency, not that the newest frame is visible.
        if (frame.frame_id < last) continue;
        const bool consistent = frame.rgba == static_cast<uint32_t>(frame.frame_id) &&
                                frame.linear_depth == static_cast<float>(frame.frame_id) * 0.5f &&
                                frame.view_proj[0] == static_cast<float>(frame.frame_id);
        if (!consistent) {
            std::printf("FAIL torn frame %llu rgba=%u depth=%.1f vp0=%.1f\n",
                        static_cast<unsigned long long>(frame.frame_id), frame.rgba,
                        frame.linear_depth, frame.view_proj[0]);
            return 1;
        }
        if (frame.frame_id > last) {
            last = frame.frame_id;
            ++accepted;
        }
    }
    writer.join();
    std::printf("PASS frames=%llu accepted=%llu reads=%llu retries=%llu\n",
                static_cast<unsigned long long>(written.load()),
                static_cast<unsigned long long>(accepted),
                static_cast<unsigned long long>(reads.load()),
                static_cast<unsigned long long>(torn.load()));
    return last == kFrames ? 0 : 1;
}

```

`encyclopedia/blueprints/ipc_seqlock.h`:

```h
// ipc_seqlock.h — single-header C++17 named shared memory for one frame header.
//
// Why: a passthrough renderer needs the guest's latest camera pose, linear depth
// and a representative RGBA value without blocking the host render thread. A
// seqlock gives a consistent snapshot without a mutex: the writer bumps an odd
// sequence before the payload and an even one after; the reader retries if the
// sequence changed or is odd. This file stores the header only (160 bytes per
// slot), not a full image. Put large pixel buffers in a separate mapping and
// send their slot id/handle in this header.
//
// Layout is fixed and asserted; keep ipc_seqlock.py in sync if you change it.
#pragma once
#ifndef WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#define WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#endif
#include <windows.h>

#include <atomic>
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstring>
#include <memory>
#include <string>
#include <type_traits>

namespace reverse_this {

constexpr uint32_t kIpcMagic = 0x50544652;  // 'RTFP' little-endian
constexpr uint32_t kIpcVersion = 1;
constexpr uint32_t kSlotCount = 3;

struct Frame {
    uint32_t rgba;          // 0xAABBGGRR, guest center/reference pixel
    float linear_depth;     // guest linear depth in meters
    float near_z;           // guest near plane
    float far_z;            // guest far plane
    float view_proj[16];    // guest camera view-projection, row-major
    float camera_world[16]; // guest camera world transform, row-major
    uint64_t frame_id;
    uint64_t timestamp_ns;
};

struct Slot {
    std::atomic<uint32_t> seq;  // even = stable, odd = write in progress
    uint32_t pad;               // keeps Frame 8-byte aligned
    Frame frame;
};

struct Shared {
    uint32_t magic;
    uint32_t version;
    uint32_t slot_count;
    uint32_t reserved;
    Slot slots[kSlotCount];
};

static_assert(std::is_trivially_copyable<Frame>::value, "Frame must be trivially copyable");
static_assert(std::atomic<uint32_t>::is_always_lock_free, "uint32_t atomics must be lock-free");
static_assert(sizeof(Frame) == 160, "Frame layout changed; update ipc_seqlock.py");
static_assert(sizeof(Slot) == 168, "Slot layout changed; update ipc_seqlock.py");
static_assert(sizeof(Shared) == 520, "Shared layout changed; update ipc_seqlock.py");

// Owns one named mapping. Create() is for the producer; Open() is for a reader.
class SharedFrameBuffer {
public:
    static std::unique_ptr<SharedFrameBuffer> Create(const std::wstring& name,
                                                     bool* already_exists = nullptr) {
        auto self = std::unique_ptr<SharedFrameBuffer>(new SharedFrameBuffer());
        self->name_ = name;
        self->mapping_ = CreateFileMappingW(INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE, nullptr, PAGE_READWRITE, 0,
                                            static_cast<DWORD>(sizeof(Shared)), name.c_str());
        if (!self->mapping_) return nullptr;
        if (already_exists) *already_exists = (GetLastError() == ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS);
        self->view_ = static_cast<Shared*>(MapViewOfFile(self->mapping_, FILE_MAP_ALL_ACCESS, 0, 0,
                                                         sizeof(Shared)));
        if (!self->view_) return nullptr;
        if (!*already_exists) {
            std::memset(self->view_, 0, sizeof(Shared));
            self->view_->magic = kIpcMagic;
            self->view_->version = kIpcVersion;
            self->view_->slot_count = kSlotCount;
        }
        if (self->view_->magic != kIpcMagic || self->view_->version != kIpcVersion ||
            self->view_->slot_count != kSlotCount) {
            return nullptr;  // incompatible producer
        }
        return self;
    }

    static std::unique_ptr<SharedFrameBuffer> Open(const std::wstring& name) {
        auto self = std::unique_ptr<SharedFrameBuffer>(new SharedFrameBuffer());
        self->name_ = name;
        self->mapping_ = OpenFileMappingW(FILE_MAP_ALL_ACCESS, FALSE, name.c_str());
        if (!self->mapping_) return nullptr;
        self->view_ = static_cast<Shared*>(MapViewOfFile(self->mapping_, FILE_MAP_ALL_ACCESS, 0, 0,
                                                         sizeof(Shared)));
        if (!self->view_ || self->view_->magic != kIpcMagic ||
            self->view_->version != kIpcVersion || self->view_->slot_count != kSlotCount) {
            return nullptr;
        }
        return self;
    }

    ~SharedFrameBuffer() {
        if (view_) UnmapViewOfFile(view_);
        if (mapping_) CloseHandle(mapping_);
    }

    SharedFrameBuffer(const SharedFrameBuffer&) = delete;
    SharedFrameBuffer& operator=(const SharedFrameBuffer&) = delete;

    // Writer: one producer only. Returns false if the slot is being read? No:
    // seqlock writers never wait for readers; they just invalidate the slot.
    bool Write(const Frame& frame) {
        if (!view_ || view_->slot_count == 0) return false;
        Slot& slot = view_->slots[frame.frame_id % view_->slot_count];
        uint32_t s = slot.seq.load(std::memory_order_relaxed);
        slot.seq.store(s + 1, std::memory_order_relaxed);  // odd: write in progress
        std::atomic_thread_fence(std::memory_order_release);
        slot.frame = frame;
        std::atomic_thread_fence(std::memory_order_release);
        slot.seq.store(s + 2, std::memory_order_relaxed);  // even: stable
        return true;
    }

    // Reader: retries a bounded number of times, then reports a torn read.
    bool ReadSlot(uint32_t index, Frame* out, int retries = 8) const {
        if (!view_ || !out || index >= view_->slot_count) return false;
        const Slot& slot = view_->slots[index];
        for (int attempt = 0; attempt < retries; ++attempt) {
            uint32_t s1 = slot.seq.load(std::memory_order_acquire);
            if (s1 & 1u) continue;
            Frame copy = slot.frame;
            std::atomic_thread_fence(std::memory_order_acquire);
            uint32_t s2 = slot.seq.load(std::memory_order_relaxed);
            if (s1 == s2) {
                *out = copy;
                return true;
            }
        }
        return false;
    }

    // Reader: newest slot by frame_id among the slots that read cleanly.
    bool ReadLatest(Frame* out) const {
        if (!view_ || !out) return false;
        bool have = false;
        Frame best{};
        for (uint32_t i = 0; i < view_->slot_count; ++i) {
            Frame candidate{};
            if (!ReadSlot(i, &candidate)) continue;
            if (!have || candidate.frame_id > best.frame_id) {
                best = candidate;
                have = true;
            }
        }
        if (have) *out = best;
        return have;
    }

    uint32_t slot_count() const { return view_ ? view_->slot_count : 0; }

private:
    SharedFrameBuffer() = default;
    HANDLE mapping_ = nullptr;
    Shared* view_ = nullptr;
    std::wstring name_;
};

}  // namespace reverse_this

```

`encyclopedia/blueprints/ipc_seqlock.py`:

```py
"""ipc_seqlock.py — reader/writer for ipc_seqlock.h on Windows x64.

Run:
    python ipc_seqlock.py            # create a demo mapping and round-trip 10 frames
    python ipc_seqlock.py --open     # read the newest frame from the demo mapping

Layout is asserted against the C++ header (Frame 160 bytes, Slot 168, Shared 520).
Python's standard library exposes no CPU fence intrinsic; on x86-64 the store order is preserved (TSO), which
is the supported target. Port the fences before using this on ARM64.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import ctypes
import mmap
import time

MAGIC = 0x50544652  # 'RTFP'
VERSION = 1
SLOTS = 3
NAME = r"Local\ReverseThisIpcDemo"


class Frame(ctypes.Structure):
    _fields_ = [
        ("rgba", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("linear_depth", ctypes.c_float),
        ("near_z", ctypes.c_float),
        ("far_z", ctypes.c_float),
        ("view_proj", ctypes.c_float * 16),
        ("camera_world", ctypes.c_float * 16),
        ("frame_id", ctypes.c_uint64),
        ("timestamp_ns", ctypes.c_uint64),
    ]


class Slot(ctypes.Structure):
    _fields_ = [
        ("seq", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("pad", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("frame", Frame),
    ]


class Shared(ctypes.Structure):
    _fields_ = [
        ("magic", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("version", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("slot_count", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("reserved", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("slots", Slot * SLOTS),
    ]


assert ctypes.sizeof(Frame) == 160, ctypes.sizeof(Frame)
assert ctypes.sizeof(Slot) == 168, ctypes.sizeof(Slot)
assert ctypes.sizeof(Shared) == 520, ctypes.sizeof(Shared)


def open_mapping(name: str = NAME, create: bool = True):
    """Return (mmap, Shared). create=True initializes a new mapping."""
    mm = mmap.mmap(-1, ctypes.sizeof(Shared), tagname=name)
    shared = Shared.from_buffer(mm)
    if create and shared.magic == 0:
        shared.magic = MAGIC
        shared.version = VERSION
        shared.slot_count = SLOTS
    if shared.magic != MAGIC or shared.version != VERSION or shared.slot_count != SLOTS:
        raise RuntimeError(f"{name} is not a compatible Reverse THIS! frame mapping")
    return mm, shared


def write_frame(shared: Shared, frame: Frame) -> None:
    """Seqlock write: odd sequence, payload, even sequence."""
    slot = shared.slots[frame.frame_id % shared.slot_count]
    seq = slot.seq
    slot.seq = seq + 1
    slot.frame = frame
    slot.seq = seq + 2


def read_latest(shared: Shared, retries: int = 8) -> Frame | None:
    """Return the newest clean snapshot, or None if every attempt was torn."""
    best: Frame | None = None
    for index in range(shared.slot_count):
        slot = shared.slots[index]
        for _ in range(retries):
            seq1 = slot.seq
            if seq1 & 1:
                continue
            raw = ctypes.string_at(ctypes.addressof(slot.frame), ctypes.sizeof(Frame))
            frame = Frame.from_buffer_copy(raw)
            if slot.seq == seq1:
                if best is None or frame.frame_id > best.frame_id:
                    best = frame
                break
    return best


def _demo() -> int:
    mm, shared = open_mapping()
    try:
        for frame_id in range(1, 11):
            frame = Frame()
            frame.frame_id = frame_id
            frame.rgba = 0xFF000000 | frame_id
            frame.linear_depth = frame_id * 0.5
            frame.near_z = 0.1
            frame.far_z = 1000.0
            frame.view_proj[0] = float(frame_id)
            frame.timestamp_ns = time.time_ns()
            write_frame(shared, frame)
        latest = read_latest(shared)
        if latest is None:
            print("FAIL no clean frame")
            return 1
        print(
            "PASS frame_id=%d rgba=0x%08X depth=%.1f vp0=%.1f"
            % (latest.frame_id, latest.rgba, latest.linear_depth, latest.view_proj[0])
        )
        return 0 if latest.frame_id == 10 else 1
    finally:
        del shared
        mm.close()


def _open_only() -> int:
    mm, shared = open_mapping(create=False)
    try:
        latest = read_latest(shared)
        if latest is None:
            print("FAIL no clean frame")
            return 1
        print(
            "frame_id=%d rgba=0x%08X depth=%.1f vp0=%.1f"
            % (latest.frame_id, latest.rgba, latest.linear_depth, latest.view_proj[0])
        )
        return 0
    finally:
        del shared
        mm.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("--open", action="store_true", help="read an existing demo mapping")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    raise SystemExit(_open_only() if args.open else _demo())

```

`encyclopedia/blueprints/passthrough_reproject.fxh`:

```fxh
// passthrough_reproject.fxh — ReShade FX template for guest->host color reprojection.
//
// LIMIT (read this first): as of the ReShade 6.x effect runtime (2026-10), an
// effect is a full-screen post-process pass and the game depth texture is
// read-only. It can read host depth and reject guest pixels behind host geometry,
// but it cannot write that depth or inject geometry into the host scene. A
// ReShade *add-on* (C++ API) can issue draw calls; a D3D11 draw/depth hook is the
// engine-level route. If a future runtime exposes writable depth, update this
// header and encyclopedia/CLAIMS.md instead of repeating the old limit.
//
// Inputs: the guest writes its latest frame header through ipc_seqlock.h and its
// color/depth through shared textures or CPU-mapped buffers. A bridge copies the
// matrices and depth into the uniforms below once per frame.

#include "ReShade.fxh"

uniform float4x4 GuestInvViewProj;
uniform float4x4 GuestCameraDelta;  // current -> previous frame
uniform float4x4 HostViewProj;
uniform float3 HostCameraPos;
uniform float HostNear < ui_min = 0.01; ui_max = 10.0; > = 0.1;
uniform float HostFar < ui_min = 10.0; ui_max = 100000.0; > = 1000.0;
uniform int HostDepthReversed < ui_type = "combo"; ui_items = "No\0Yes\0"; > = 1;
uniform float PoseLag < ui_min = 0.0; ui_max = 1.0; > = 0.0;
uniform float OcclusionBias < ui_min = 0.0; ui_max = 0.1; > = 0.002;
uniform float AmbientStrength < ui_min = 0.0; ui_max = 1.0; > = 0.15;
uniform int RaySteps < ui_min = 1; ui_max = 64; > = 16;
uniform float4 GuestTint < ui_type = "color"; > = float4(1, 1, 1, 1);

// Host depth: ReShade binds the game's depth buffer to DEPTH.
texture DepthBuffer { Width = BUFFER_WIDTH; Height = BUFFER_HEIGHT; Format = R32F; };
sampler DepthBufferSampler { Texture = DepthBuffer; };

// Guest color and depth are shared textures bound by the bridge. If the bridge
// uses a CPU RGBA copy instead, replace these with 1x1 textures updated per frame.
texture GuestColorTex { Width = BUFFER_WIDTH; Height = BUFFER_HEIGHT; Format = RGBA8; };
sampler GuestColorSampler { Texture = GuestColorTex; };
texture GuestDepthTex { Width = BUFFER_WIDTH; Height = BUFFER_HEIGHT; Format = R32F; };
sampler GuestDepthSampler { Texture = GuestDepthTex; };

float LinearizeHostDepth(float raw) {
    // Standard: z = near*far / (far - raw*(far-near)).
    // Reversed:  z = near*far / (near + raw*(far-near)).
    float denom = HostDepthReversed
        ? (HostNear + raw * (HostFar - HostNear))
        : (HostFar - raw * (HostFar - HostNear));
    return (HostNear * HostFar) / max(denom, 1e-6);
}

float3 GuestWorldFromUv(float2 uv, float raw_depth) {
    float2 ndc = uv * 2.0 - 1.0;
    float4 clip = float4(ndc, raw_depth, 1.0);
    float4 world = mul(GuestInvViewProj, clip);
    return world.xyz / max(world.w, 1e-6);
}

float2 HostUvFromWorld(float3 world) {
    float4 clip = mul(HostViewProj, float4(world, 1.0));
    float2 ndc = clip.xy / max(clip.w, 1e-6);
    return ndc * 0.5 + 0.5;
}

// One-frame pose lag: GuestCameraDelta maps the current world point back to the
// previous frame. PoseLag = 1 uses the previous position; 0 uses the current.
float3 ApplyPoseLag(float3 world) {
    if (PoseLag <= 0.0) return world;
    float3 previous = mul(GuestCameraDelta, float4(world, 1.0)).xyz;
    return lerp(world, previous, PoseLag);
}

// Ray-march the host depth buffer between the host camera and the guest point.
// This is a screen-space approximation; it does not occlude the host with guest
// geometry and it is not a substitute for a real depth-tested draw.
bool OccludedByHost(float3 guest_world) {
    float3 delta = guest_world - HostCameraPos;
    float distance = length(delta);
    if (distance <= 0.001) return false;
    float3 dir = delta / distance;
    float step_size = distance / float(max(RaySteps, 1));
    [loop] for (int i = 1; i <= RaySteps; ++i) {
        float t = step_size * float(i);
        float2 uv = HostUvFromWorld(HostCameraPos + dir * t);
        if (any(uv < 0.0) || any(uv > 1.0)) continue;
        float host_depth = LinearizeHostDepth(tex2D(DepthBufferSampler, uv).r);
        if (host_depth > 0.0 && host_depth < t - OcclusionBias) return true;
    }
    return false;
}

float4 PS_Passthrough(float4 pos : SV_Position, float2 uv : TEXCOORD) : SV_Target {
    float4 guest = tex2D(GuestColorSampler, uv);
    if (guest.a <= 0.001) return 0.0;

    float raw_guest = tex2D(GuestDepthSampler, uv).r;
    float3 guest_world = GuestWorldFromUv(uv, raw_guest);
    guest_world = ApplyPoseLag(guest_world);

    if (OccludedByHost(guest_world)) return 0.0;

    float2 host_uv = HostUvFromWorld(guest_world);
    if (any(host_uv < 0.0) || any(host_uv > 1.0)) return 0.0;

    float3 host_color = tex2D(ReShade::BackBuffer, host_uv).rgb;
    float host_luma = dot(host_color, float3(0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722));
    float3 tinted = guest.rgb * GuestTint.rgb * lerp(1.0, host_luma, AmbientStrength);
    return float4(tinted, guest.a);
}

void VS_Passthrough(in uint id : SV_VertexID, out float4 pos : SV_POSITION, out float2 uv : TEXCOORD) {
    uv = float2((id << 1) & 2, id & 2);
    pos = float4(uv * float2(2, -2) + float2(-1, 1), 0, 1);
}

technique PassthroughReproject < ui_label = "Passthrough reprojection"; >
{
    pass
    {
        VertexShader = VS_Passthrough;
        PixelShader = PS_Passthrough;
    }
}

```

`encyclopedia/blueprints/proxy_entity_bridge.md`:

```md
# Proxy entity bridge (cross-engine combat and collision)

Use this when two runtimes must share collision and combat but neither engine can host the other's actors. The bridge is a small authority layer, not a second game.

## Topology

```
Guest (engine A)                         Host (engine B)
  guest player/camera                      host player/camera
  guest physics/collision                  host physics/collision
        |                                        |
        +---- WebSocket 127.0.0.1:<port> --------+
                     JSON messages
```

- One process owns the **authority** for a given interaction. Do not let both engines apply damage or impulse to the same event.
- The bridge is loopback-only. Bind `127.0.0.1`, require a token, and reject non-loopback peers.
- Tick order: host physics -> bridge exchange -> guest physics -> render. A frame of lag is expected; interpolate, do not teleport.

## Message schema

```json
{
  "v": 1,
  "tick": 12345,
  "kind": "raycast | block | damage | pose | hello",
  "id": "guest-entity-7",
  "pos": [0.0, 0.0, 0.0],
  "rot": [0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0],
  "hit": {"point": [0.0, 0.0, 0.0], "normal": [0.0, 0.0, 1.0], "distance": 1.2},
  "damage": {"amount": 10.0, "type": "blunt", "source": "host-entity-3"},
  "flags": {"authority": "host", "hidden": true}
}
```

Rules:

1. `tick` is monotonic per sender; drop out-of-order messages.
2. `id` is namespaced (`guest-`, `host-`) so an id collision cannot silently bind the wrong actor.
3. `pos`/`rot` are already converted to the receiver's coordinate system by the sender's adapter. Do not convert twice.
4. `damage` carries `authority`; the receiver applies it only when it is not the authority.
5. Unknown `kind` is ignored and logged once, not fatal.

## Floor raycasts -> invisible barrier blocks

For a hollow guest building shell:

1. Cast down from the guest player every tick (or every N ticks) against the host collision world.
2. If the hit distance is below the guest floor height, spawn or move an invisible host collision block under the player.
3. Keep a small pool (8-16 blocks) and recycle; do not spawn per frame.
4. Despawn blocks when the player leaves the tile or after a timeout.

## Guest block placement -> host collision props

When the guest places a block:

1. Guest sends `kind: "block"` with the grid cell and material.
2. Host spawns a collision prop at the converted position, with `flags.hidden = true` and no render mesh.
3. Host acknowledges with the prop id; the guest stores the mapping.
4. On removal, host despawns the prop and acknowledges. If the ack is missing for two ticks, retry once, then log and drop.

## Invisible proxy entities and damage interception

- Spawn one host proxy entity per guest combatant. Give it an infinite hidden status effect (or the engine's equivalent) so it never dies or renders.
- The proxy carries the guest entity id in its name/metadata.
- Host damage events are intercepted before application:
  1. Read the attacker, target and damage type.
  2. Convert the target proxy id back to the guest entity id.
  3. Send `kind: "damage"` to the guest with `authority: "guest"`.
  4. Cancel the host application if the guest owns health.
- Guest health changes are sent back as `kind: "pose"`/`kind: "damage"` state updates; the host only displays them.

## Failure modes

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Proxy jitters between two positions | Both engines apply the same movement | Pick one authority per entity and make the other read-only |
| Damage applies twice | Host applies before forwarding | Intercept and cancel before the host health write |
| Proxy dies and respawns | No hidden/infinite status effect | Reapply the effect on spawn and on respawn |
| Blocks drift from the guest grid | Position converted twice | Convert once at the adapter boundary; log the raw and converted values |
| Bridge stalls the render thread | Synchronous socket call in `Present` | Move I/O to a worker thread; publish snapshots through a seqlock |

## Verification

- A raycast from the guest hits the host floor at the expected height.
- One block placed in the guest appears as a host collision prop at the same world point.
- One damage event crosses the bridge once, not twice.
- Kill the bridge: both engines keep running and log the disconnect; no hang.

```

`encyclopedia/engines/creation-engine.md`:

```md
# Creation Engine (Skyrim SE/AE, Fallout 4, Starfield)

## Fingerprint

| Signal | Creation Engine 1 (Skyrim LE/SE) | Creation Engine 2 (Fallout 4, Starfield) |
|---|---|---|
| Executable | `SkyrimSE.exe` (SE/AE), `Skyrim.exe` (LE) | `Fallout4.exe`, `Starfield.exe` |
| Archives | `.bsa` | `.ba2` |
| Plugins | `.esp`, `.esm`, `.esl` | `.esp`, `.esm`, `.esl` |
| Script extender | `skse64_loader.exe`, `skse64_*.dll` | `f4se_loader.exe`, `sfse_loader.exe` |
| Scripts | `.pex` (Papyrus) | `.pex` (Papyrus) |
| Data root | `Data\` | `Data\` |

`.bsa` and `.ba2` are different formats. Do not feed a BA2 to a BSA reader or vice versa.

## Coordinates

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Handedness | Right-handed |
| Up axis | `+Z` |
| Forward | `+Y` (engine); actors face `+Y` at yaw 0 |
| Unit scale | 64 units = 1 yard; 1 unit = 0.5625 in = 1.42875 cm; 128 units ≈ 1.8288 m |
| Rotations | Plugin records use degrees; NIF transforms use radians/quaternions. Convert at the boundary, not inside the math. |

Conversion to a Y-up meter runtime:

```
X_out =  X_ce * 0.0142875
Y_out =  Z_ce * 0.0142875
Z_out = -Y_ce * 0.0142875
```

## Depth

Skyrim SE and Fallout 4 render with a standard D3D11 depth range (`0.0` near, `1.0` far). Do not assume reversed-Z. Confirm with a depth readback before using any reprojection math.

## Loaders and proxy DLLs

| Name | Use | Conflict |
|---|---|---|
| `dinput8.dll` | Common ASI/proxy host | Some input mods also claim it |
| `winmm.dll` | Proxy host, rarely used by the game | Audio capture overlays |
| `version.dll` | Proxy host | Version-check shims |
| `dxgi.dll` | ReShade / overlay host | ENB and other DXGI hooks |
| `d3d11.dll` | ENB host | ReShade and DXGI overlays |

SKSE is not a proxy DLL. It launches the game and injects; do not stack a second loader on the same executable without checking the log.

## IAT hook rules

1. Resolve the target module by full path, not by name alone.
2. Hook only the functions you need, and only in the target process.
3. Match `CreateFileW` by normalized path (`Data\*.bsa`, `Data\*.ba2`) and preserve every flag.
4. Never strip `FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING`: unbuffered I/O requires sector-aligned buffers and offsets, and changing the flag can turn a working read into `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`.
5. Archive warming is bounded: read the header and the blocks the cell needs. Do not map a multi-gigabyte archive to "warm" it.

## Verification

- `skse64.log` / `f4se.log` shows the plugin loaded and the runtime version matches.
- A downstream sink fires on the first archive read (log the path and byte count).
- One cell or interior loads, saves, and reloads without a crash.
- Disable the hook and confirm the original behavior returns.

## Known traps

- AE versus SE runtime offsets differ; a plugin built for 1.5.97 requires a matching address library to load on 1.6.x.
- Loose files win over archives; a "missing" asset may be an override, not a failed read.
- `Data\` may be virtualized by a mod manager; resolve the real path from the running process.

```

`encyclopedia/engines/pokemon-switch.md`:

```md
# Pokémon Switch titles — engine and native-port route

## Engine fingerprint

*Sword/Shield*, *Scarlet/Violet*, *Legends: Arceus* and *Legends: Z-A* are
Game Freak's proprietary Switch titles. They are **not Unity** and **not
Unreal Engine**; AssetRipper, FModel, UABEANext and UE4SS do not convert them.
Their containers are NSP/XCI and their RomFS trees carry Pokémon-specific
formats such as `TRMDL`, `TRMSH`, `TRMBF`, `TRSKL`, `TRANIM`, `BNTX` and
game-data archives. Verify the specific build's tree before assuming a path.

## Native-port reality

No supported emulator-to-native converter exists. A Windows version is a new
project that reuses extracted assets and reimplements the game systems.

| Engine | Use it when | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Godot 4.x | Default for a stylised Pokémon-scale fusion; light editor, C#, fast iteration. | Rebuild battle, spawn, UI and save systems. |
| Unity | You need mature FBX/glTF and character/animation tooling. | Larger editor and project; project/licence terms apply. |
| Unreal Engine 5 | High-fidelity rendering, large worlds or a team pipeline justify it. | Heavy editor and material/shader rebuild; slower iteration. |

For most fusions, Godot or Unity is more efficient than UE5. Engine choice does
not make the port automatic.

## Working pipeline

1. `nstool -k <keys> --fstree <game.nsp>`; extract the NSP and the matching
   NCA/RomFS with its ticket. Keep the original dump read-only.
2. `switch-toolbox` for BNTX textures and common formats. Its TRMDL viewing is
   incomplete for Scarlet/Violet (upstream issue #658).
3. The [Pokémon Switch Model Importer/Exporter for Blender](https://github.com/ChicoEevee/Pokemon-Switch-Model-Importer-Blender)
   imports TRMDL/TRMSH/TRMBF and animations in Blender 4.x/5.x. Z-A models were
   not working per its README on 2026-11-01. Export FBX/glTF and rebuild
   materials in the target engine.
4. `pkNX` edits Sword/Shield data; Scarlet/Violet and Z-A are dump-only in its
   current README. `PKHeX` is a save editor, not an asset converter.
5. Test asset swaps in the emulator with a LayeredFS `romfs` mod before
   rebuilding them natively.
6. Build one native project with a merged, data-driven species/move/encounter
   schema. A fusion is not two executables glued together.

The full guide, including tool status and limits, is
[docs/pokemon-switch.md](../../docs/pokemon-switch.md).

## Coordinates

The Blender importer applies the game's model orientation; do not assume Unity
or Unreal axes. Export a test model, record the axis/scale transform used, and
verify it in the target engine before batch conversion.

## Depth

These workflows are asset and data ports. They do not need a runtime depth
buffer hook; that is only relevant to rendering-injection projects.

## Verification

- Confirm the title ID/build before extraction.
- Import one model, one texture and one animation before converting a roster.
- Record game version, tool version and conversion path. A successful
  extraction is not a successful native port.

## Scope

Use dumps and keys only for games you own. Do not redistribute ROMs, keys,
extracted assets or converted asset packs.

```

# RAGE / GTA V (Legacy and Enhanced)

## Fingerprint

| Signal | Legacy | Enhanced |
|---|---|---|
| Executable | `GTA5.exe` | `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` |
| Build reference | Legacy build `3889` (verify per install) | Enhanced build numbers move independently |
| Archives | `.rpf` (`x64a.rpf`, `update\update.rpf`) | `.rpf` |
| Anti-cheat | None in single-player | BattlEye |
| Script loader | ScriptHookV (`dinput8.dll` or `xinput1_4.dll` per build), ASI loader | ScriptHookV build matching the Enhanced edition |
| Graphics overlays | ReShade (`dxgi.dll` / `d3d11.dll`), ENB | Same, but verify the proxy slot |

The Enhanced edition is not a drop-in replacement for Legacy. A ScriptHookV build, ASI plugin or offset table is not binary-compatible across editions; use the matching edition's build.

## Coordinates

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Handedness | Right-handed |
| Up axis | `+Z` |
| Forward | `+Y` |
| Unit scale | 1 unit ≈ 1 meter |
| Rotations | Heading/yaw in degrees; pitch and roll around the local axes |

Conversion to a Y-up meter runtime:

```
X_out =  X_gta
Y_out =  Z_gta
Z_out = -Y_gta
```

## Depth

GTA V uses a reversed-Z depth buffer (`1.0` near, `0.0` far). ReShade's GTA V depth fix and community depth guides confirm it. Re-read the depth buffer after a graphics update; do not hard-code the polarity.

## Loaders and proxy DLLs

| Name | Use | Conflict |
|---|---|---|
| `dinput8.dll` | ScriptHookV / ASI host | Input mods |
| `xinput1_4.dll` | ScriptHookV host on some builds | Controller remappers |
| `dxgi.dll` | ReShade / overlay host | Other DXGI hooks |
| `d3d11.dll` | ReShade / ENB host | Other D3D11 hooks |
| `winmm.dll` | Proxy host | Audio overlays |

`-nobattleye` disables BattlEye for single-player only. It is not an online route, and it does not make a modified client safe to take online. Do not test a Tier 4-6 hook with BattlEye active.

## Verification

- `ScriptHookV.log` and the ASI loader log show the plugin loaded.
- A downstream sink fires (`Present`, a UI notification, or a frame counter).
- The script spawns one object, reads its position back, and removes it.
- Disable the hook and confirm the original behavior returns.

## Known traps

- Rockstar updates replace the executable; every offset and pattern must be re-resolved.
- ReShade and ENB can both claim `dxgi.dll`/`d3d11.dll`; only one owns the slot.
- `update\update.rpf` overrides the base archives; edit the update archive when the asset is patched there.

```md
# Unity Mono / IL2CPP

## Fingerprint

| Signal | Mono | IL2CPP |
|---|---|---|
| Player | `UnityPlayer.dll` | `UnityPlayer.dll` |
| Runtime | `MonoBleedingEdge\` | `GameAssembly.dll` |
| Game code | `*_Data\Managed\Assembly-CSharp.dll` | `GameAssembly.dll` + `*_Data\il2cpp_data\Metadata\global-metadata.dat` |
| Managed assemblies | Many `.dll` files | Stripped; metadata is the map |
| Loader | BepInEx 5, BepInEx 6 (pre-release), MelonLoader | MelonLoader, BepInEx 6 IL2CPP (pre-release), Il2CppInterop |
| Inspector | UnityExplorer (BepInEx/MelonLoader builds) | UnityExplorer MelonLoader IL2CPP build |

`MonoBleedingEdge` plus `Assembly-CSharp.dll` means Mono. `GameAssembly.dll` plus `il2cpp_data` means IL2CPP. Do not install a Mono plugin into an IL2CPP game.

## Coordinates

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Handedness | Left-handed |
| Up axis | `+Y` |
| Forward | `+Z` |
| Unit scale | 1 Unity unit = 1 meter |
| Rotations | Quaternions; `Transform.eulerAngles` is degrees, yaw around Y |

Conversion to a Z-up centimeter runtime:

```
X_out =  X_unity * 100
Y_out = -Z_unity * 100
Z_out =  Y_unity * 100
```

## Depth

Unity's D3D11/D3D12/Vulkan renderers commonly use a reversed-Z depth buffer; some paths and older renderers use standard depth. Read the depth texture and test near/far before writing reprojection math.

## Loaders

| Loader | Runtime | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| BepInEx 5 | Mono | Current stable line for Mono games |
| BepInEx 6 | Mono / IL2CPP | Pre-release; pin the exact build |
| MelonLoader | Mono / IL2CPP | Current x64 zip; IL2CPP support is the reason it is here |
| UnityExplorer | Matching loader/runtime | In-game scene inspector, not a loader |

Copy the loader into the game folder; do not replace `UnityPlayer.dll` unless the loader explicitly documents that route.

## Harmony patterns

```csharp
[HarmonyPatch(typeof(PlayerController), nameof(PlayerController.Update))]
static class PlayerControllerUpdatePatch
{
    static void Prefix(PlayerController __instance) { /* before original */ }
    static void Postfix(PlayerController __instance) { /* after original */ }
    static IEnumerable<CodeInstruction> Transpiler(IEnumerable<CodeInstruction> instructions)
    {
        // Rewrite IL only after dumping the original instructions.
        return instructions;
    }
}
```

Rules:

1. Prefix/postfix first; a transpiler is the last resort.
2. Dump the original IL before rewriting it; keep a diff.
3. Guard every patch with a config toggle and a try/catch that logs once.
4. Re-check the patch after every game update; managed method signatures move.

## Proxy DLLs

| Name | Use | Conflict |
|---|---|---|
| `winmm.dll` | Proxy host | Audio overlays |
| `version.dll` | Proxy host | Version shims |
| `dinput8.dll` | Proxy host | Input mods |
| `dxgi.dll` | ReShade / overlay | Other DXGI hooks |

## Verification

- BepInEx console or `BepInEx\LogOutput.log` shows the plugin loaded.
- UnityExplorer shows the patched object or the spawned proxy.
- A downstream sink fires (UI text, frame counter, or `Present`).
- Disable the plugin and confirm the original behavior returns.

`encyclopedia/engines/unreal-3-4-5.md`:

```md
# Unreal Engine 3 / 4 / 5

## Fingerprint

| Signal | UE3 | UE4 / UE5 |
|---|---|---|
| Executable | `Binaries\Win32\*.exe`, `Binaries\Win64\*.exe` | `Binaries\Win64\*.exe`, `*-Win64-Shipping.exe` |
| Cooked data | `.upk`, `.u`, `CookedPC*` | `.pak`, `.utoc` + `.ucas` (IoStore) |
| Project files | `.ini`, `.int` | `.uproject` (editor/source), `.ini` |
| Scripting | UnrealScript (cooked) | Blueprints (cooked), C++ |
| Loader | UE4SS (UE4/5), SDK dumpers | UE4SS (experimental), FModel, repak, retoc |
| Physics | PhysX | PhysX (UE4) / Chaos (UE5) |

UE3 packages and UE4/5 packages are not interchangeable. A `.upk` is not a `.pak`.

## Coordinates

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Handedness | Left-handed |
| Up axis | `+Z` |
| Forward | `+X` |
| Unit scale | UE4/5: 1 Unreal Unit = 1 cm. UE3: often 1 UU = 1 cm, but titles rescale; verify against a known actor. |
| Rotations | Yaw around Z, pitch around Y, roll around X; degrees in Blueprint/INI, radians in some native APIs |

Conversion to a Y-up meter runtime:

```
X_out =  X_ue * 0.01
Y_out =  Z_ue * 0.01
Z_out = -Y_ue * 0.01
```

UE is left-handed; a straight axis swap without the sign change mirrors the world. Test with a known landmark before shipping.

## Depth

UE4/5 default to a reversed-Z depth buffer (`1.0` near, `0.0` far) with a floating-point depth target. UE3 titles vary; read the depth buffer and test a near/far sample before writing reprojection math.

## Loaders and proxy DLLs

| Name | Use | Conflict |
|---|---|---|
| `dinput8.dll` | UE4SS and ASI hosts | Input mods |
| `winmm.dll` | Proxy host | Audio overlays |
| `xinput1_3.dll` / `xinput1_4.dll` | Proxy host, controller shims | Controller remappers |
| `dwmapi.dll` | Proxy host | Desktop composition overlays |
| `dxgi.dll` | ReShade / overlay host | Other DXGI hooks |

UE4SS ships as a set of DLLs and Lua scripts; copy the whole folder, not just `UE4SS.dll`.

## Hollow-shell streaming

For a hollow building shell, do not cook a second collision world. Stream collision tiles around the player:

1. Raycast down from the guest camera to find the floor.
2. Spawn host collision blocks for the tiles within the streaming radius.
3. Despawn tiles behind the player; keep a one-tile margin.
4. Re-run the floor raycast after every teleport; a stale floor height is the usual "falling through the world" bug.

PhysX (UE4) and Chaos (UE5) expose different trace APIs. A collision shape that works in one may be ignored by the other.

## Verification

- UE4SS log shows the Lua mod loaded and the SDK dump completed.
- A downstream sink fires (`Present`, a UI text, or an actor spawn log).
- One actor spawns, collides, and is removed without a crash.
- Disable the mod and confirm the original behavior returns.

```

`encyclopedia/games/arkham-knight-x-spiderman.md`:

```md
# Arkham Knight x Spider-Man

## Identity

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Host game / store edition | Spider-Man (Insomniac engine), Steam |
| Guest game / store edition | Batman: Arkham Knight, Steam `v4.0630` |
| Host build id | not recorded |
| Guest build id | `v4.0630` (user-provided) |
| Host EXE SHA256 | not recorded |
| Guest EXE SHA256 | not recorded |
| Loader / proxy DLL | Host `dinput8.dll`; guest `winmm.dll` |
| Loader version | not recorded |
| Toolchain | not recorded |
| Date verified | 2026-10-31 (design review only) |
| Owner / workspace | local lab |

## Coordinate conversion

Arkham Knight is UE3: Z-up, left-handed, 1 Unreal Unit = 1 cm (verify per build). The Insomniac host is Y-up, meters.

Matrix (Arkham -> host):

```
X_h =  X_ak * 0.01
Y_h =  Z_ak * 0.01
Z_h = -Y_ak * 0.01
```

Yaw (UE yaw is around +Z; host yaw is around +Y):

```
Yaw_h = -Yaw_ak
```

Known landmark: the clock tower roof edge. Record the guest and host positions before trusting the matrix.

## IPC

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Mapping name | `Local\ArkWeb_v1` |
| Header version | 1 |
| Slots | 3 (seqlock, `ipc_seqlock.h`) |
| Frame fields | RGBA, linear depth, near/far, guest view-projection, camera world, frame id |
| Bridge transport | WebSocket `127.0.0.1` for commands; shared memory for per-frame pose/depth |

## Authority matrix

| Interaction | Authority | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| Traversal (swing, wall-run, grapple) | Host | Guest camera follows host root; guest animation is cosmetic |
| Freeflow combat | Guest | Host renders proxy entities and forwards damage |
| Bone scaling (combat hit reactions) | Guest during Freeflow, Host during traversal | Swap on a state transition, not per frame |
| Collision blocks | Host | Guest sends block placement; host spawns hidden collision props |
| Camera | Host during traversal, Guest during combat | Blend for 0.25 s at the swap; never snap |

The bone-scaling authority swap is the fragile part. Gate it on an explicit combat state, not on animation names. On swap, send the current pose and scale once, then let the new authority drive.

## Command log and floor clamp

`logs\ak_cmd.txt` is the guest command log. Parse the last command per tick and clamp the guest Z to the host floor height plus a margin:

```
z_guest = max(z_guest, host_floor_z + 0.05)
```

The clamp prevents the guest capsule from falling through a host-only floor during a traversal-to-combat transition. Log every clamp; a silent clamp hides a conversion bug.

## Known engine traps

- UE3 cooked packages do not expose source names; a hook on a wrapper may not run in a cooked build.
- The guest and host may both apply root motion during the swap frame; disable one for that frame.
- Insomniac's host uses meters; a 100x scale error looks like "the camera is inside the floor".
- `winmm.dll` and `dinput8.dll` must be loaded before the guest's first frame; a late load loses the first pose.

## Verification evidence

| Check | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Offline IPC harness | pending | `ipc_link_test.exe` |
| Reversed-Z pixel test | pending | `fake_host_d3d11.exe` |
| Downstream sink fires | pending | `Present` counter |
| One traversal | pending | |
| One combat exchange | pending | |
| Save / reload | pending | |

## Rollback

- Backups: guest save folder copied before the first run.
- Disable order: stop the bridge, remove `winmm.dll`, remove `dinput8.dll`, restore saves.
- Last known good: not recorded.

```

`encyclopedia/games/gtav-x-minecraft-passthrough.md`:

```md
# GTA V x Minecraft Java (Fabric)

## Identity

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Host game / store edition | GTA V Legacy, Steam/Rockstar, build `3889` (user-provided) |
| Guest game / store edition | Minecraft Java, Fabric loader |
| Host build id | `3889` |
| Guest build id | not recorded |
| Host EXE SHA256 | not recorded |
| Guest EXE SHA256 | not recorded |
| Loader / proxy DLL | Host `dinput8.dll` (ScriptHookV) or `xinput1_4.dll`; ReShade `dxgi.dll` |
| Loader version | not recorded |
| Toolchain | ScriptHookV, ASI, Fabric, ReShade |
| Date verified | 2026-10-31 (design review only) |
| Owner / workspace | local lab |

The Enhanced edition is `GTA5_Enhanced.exe` with BattlEye; this note covers the Legacy single-player build. Never take a modified client online.

## Coordinate conversion

GTA V: Z-up, right-handed, 1 unit ≈ 1 meter. Minecraft: Y-up, right-handed, 1 block = 1 meter.

Matrix (GTA -> Minecraft):

```
X_mc =  X_gta
Y_mc =  Z_gta + Y_offset
Z_mc = -Y_gta
```

`Y_offset` is the host floor height at the conversion origin; record it per session.

Yaw:

```
Yaw_mc = 180 - Heading_gta
```

Normalize to `[-180, 180]`. Test with the player facing north and east; a single-point test will not catch a sign flip.

## IPC

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Mapping name | `Local\MCPassthroughFrame` |
| Header version | 1 |
| Slots | 3 (seqlock, `ipc_seqlock.h`) |
| Frame fields | RGBA, linear depth, near/far, view-projection, camera world, frame id |
| Bridge transport | Shared memory for pose/depth; loopback WebSocket for commands |

## ReShade composite

GTA V uses a reversed-Z depth buffer. In the shader set `HostDepthReversed = 1`; the host linearization is `near*far / (near + raw*(far-near))`. The guest (Minecraft) depth texture must use the same clip convention as the supplied `GuestInvViewProj`; verify that pair with `fake_host_d3d11.exe` before shipping.

The composite is a post-process: it can reject guest pixels behind GTA geometry, but an effect cannot write GTA's depth buffer; an add-on or a draw hook is the current route. Re-test after a ReShade runtime change.

## Authority matrix

| Interaction | Authority | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| Player position | GTA (host) | Minecraft camera follows the host root |
| Block placement | Minecraft (guest) | Host spawns hidden collision props |
| Damage | Guest for guest entities, host for host entities | Never apply the same event twice |
| Vehicle physics | GTA | Guest is a passenger; no guest collision |
| Camera | GTA during driving, guest on foot | Blend at the transition |

## Known engine traps

- GTA heading and Minecraft yaw use different zero points and signs; the `180 - heading` formula assumes both are degrees.
- `update\update.rpf` overrides base archives; edit the update archive when the asset is patched there.
- ScriptHookV must match the exact build; a mismatch fails silently in `ScriptHookV.log`.
- ReShade and ENB can both claim `dxgi.dll`/`d3d11.dll`; only one owns the slot.
- Minecraft's chunk origin is not the player origin; convert the entity position, not the chunk coordinate.

## Verification evidence

| Check | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Offline IPC harness | pending | `ipc_link_test.exe` |
| Reversed-Z pixel test | pending | `fake_host_d3d11.exe` |
| Downstream sink fires | pending | `Present` counter |
| One traversal | pending | |
| One block placement | pending | |
| Save / reload | pending | |

## Rollback

- Backups: GTA save folder and Minecraft world copied before the first run.
- Disable order: stop the bridge, remove the ASI/ScriptHookV DLLs, remove ReShade, restore saves.
- Last known good: not recorded.

```

`encyclopedia/re-recipes/aob-wildcard-signatures.md`:

```md
# AOB wildcard signatures

An Array-of-Bytes signature is a byte pattern with wildcards for bytes that move between builds. The goal is a pattern that is unique, ASLR-safe and survives compiler/layout changes.

## What moves, what stays

| Bytes | Stability | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Opcode + ModRM | Usually stable | Keep |
| RIP-relative `disp32` | Moves with layout | Wildcard all 4 bytes |
| `rel8`/`rel32` branch targets | Move with layout | Wildcard 1 or 4 bytes |
| Absolute addresses (imm64) | Move with ASLR/rebuild | Wildcard |
| Stack offsets, struct offsets | Change with code changes | Keep only if the build is pinned |
| Function prologue/epilogue | Often stable | Good anchor |

Do not wildcard the opcode or the ModRM; that turns a signature into a loose string and produces false positives.

## Algorithm

1. Read the PE, map `RVA -> file offset` from the section table.
2. Find the candidate byte pattern in the `.text` section (or at the given RVA).
3. Disassemble a window (16-32 instructions) with Capstone.
4. For each instruction, mask the displacement/immediate bytes:
   - `call rel32`, `jmp rel32`, `jcc rel32` -> last 4 bytes
   - `call rel8`, `jmp rel8`, `jcc rel8` -> last 1 byte
   - `mov reg, [rip+disp32]` -> last 4 bytes of the instruction
   - `mov reg, imm64` -> last 8 bytes
5. Emit `48 8B 05 ?? ?? ?? ?? 48 85 C0` with `??` for masked bytes.
6. Verify the pattern is unique in the module and that it resolves at the same address in a second build.

## Reference implementation

`kit/aob_gen.py` does steps 1-5 with the standard library plus Capstone. It takes a file, an RVA or a hex pattern, and a window size:

```powershell
aob-gen "C:\Games\Target\Target.exe" --rva 0x1A2B30 --instructions 24
aob-gen "C:\Games\Target\Target.exe" --pattern "48 8B 05 ?? ?? ?? ??" --instructions 24
```

Output is JSON: `{"pattern": "...", "rva": "...", "bytes": N, "wildcards": N, "unique": true}`. `unique` is checked against the whole file; if false, extend the window.

## Validation

- The signature matches exactly once in the current build.
- It matches exactly once in the previous build (if available).
- The resolved address lands on the same instruction, not a nearby function.
- A patched byte in the wildcarded region does not break the match; a patched opcode does.
- Record the build id, file hash and the signature in the field note. A signature without its build is a guess.

## Failure modes

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Multiple matches | Window too short or too generic | Extend the window; add a unique prologue |
| No match after an update | Opcode or layout changed | Re-derive from the new build; do not widen blindly |
| Match lands mid-instruction | Pattern started at an operand | Anchor at a function prologue or a known instruction boundary |
| Works in IDA, not in the injector | Injector scans mapped memory, not the file | Use the module's mapped image and the correct section protections |

```

`encyclopedia/re-recipes/frida-downstream-sink.md`:

```md
# Frida downstream-sink synthesis

Use this when a static finding needs runtime proof and the obvious wrapper hook stays silent.

## Static -> dynamic hook

1. In Ghidra, find the leaf function that touches the target data (the one that reads/writes the field, not the public wrapper).
2. Record the module base-relative RVA, the calling convention and the argument registers.
3. In Frida, resolve the module by name and attach at `base + rva`:

```javascript
const mod = Process.getModuleByName("Target.exe");
const fn = mod.base.add(0x1A2B30);
Interceptor.attach(fn, {
  onEnter(args) {
    this.obj = args[0];
    console.log("enter", this.obj, Thread.backtrace(this.context, Backtrace.FunctionType.FULL).map(DebugSymbol.fromAddress).join("\n"));
  },
  onLeave(retval) {
    console.log("leave", retval);
  }
});
```

4. If the hook never fires, the leaf is not on the path you think. Move to the downstream sink.

## Downstream Sink Protocol

1. Pick a sink that must run every frame or every UI update: `IDXGISwapChain::Present`, `EndScene`, a UI text draw, or a frame counter.
2. Attach a counter and a backtrace at the sink:

```javascript
const present = Module.getExportByName("dxgi.dll", "Present");
Interceptor.attach(present, {
  onEnter() {
    this.n = (this.n || 0) + 1;
    if (this.n % 60 === 0) {
      console.log("frame", this.n);
      console.log(Thread.backtrace(this.context, Backtrace.FunctionType.FULL)
        .slice(0, 12).map(DebugSymbol.fromAddress).join("\n"));
    }
  }
});
```

3. Walk the backtrace from the sink until you find the producer that should have called your hook.
4. Hook the producer's leaf, not its wrapper or constructor. Wrapper/constructor hooks commonly run on a different thread or a copy of the object.
5. Once the hook fires, log a payload hash and the frame number. That is the canary.

## When the sink itself is silent

- The game may use a different swap chain (DX12 `Present` on `IDXGISwapChain3/4`, Vulkan `vkQueuePresentKHR`, or a launcher's overlay chain).
- The process may render in a child process; enumerate processes, not just the main module.
- The overlay may hook before you; check the import table and the loaded modules for other overlays.

## Runtime dumping (PyArmor example)

PyArmor-protected Python ships `pyarmor_runtime.pyd/.so` plus encrypted code objects. At runtime the code objects are decrypted in memory.

1. Attach to the process, enumerate modules, find `pyarmor_runtime`.
2. Locate the decrypted code object by scanning for `PyCodeObject` structures or by hooking `PyEval_EvalCode`/`exec`.
3. Dump the `co_code`, `co_consts` and `co_names` for the target module; write them to a lab directory.
4. Record the PyArmor and Python versions; the structure and the decryption path change between releases.

Caveats: native extensions and anti-debug checks can defeat a naive dump; a dumped code object is not original source; and the target must be one you own or are authorized to analyze. Keep the dump and the target out of releases.

## Verification

- The sink counter advances.
- The leaf hook fires with the expected argument values.
- A known field changes at the expected time.
- Disable the hook and confirm the original behavior returns.

```

`encyclopedia/re-recipes/ghidra-mcp-streamable-http.md`:

```md
# Ghidra MCP over Streamable HTTP

## Ports (do not conflate them)

| Process | Default | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| GhidraMCP plugin | `127.0.0.1:8080` | Ghidra-side HTTP bridge (REST-style endpoints) |
| MCP bridge | `127.0.0.1:8081` | MCP server, transport `streamable-http`, endpoint `/mcp` |

The MCP client talks to `8081/mcp`, not `8080/mcp`. The bridge talks to the Ghidra plugin on `8080`. Verify each layer separately.

This recipe is optional and agent-run. The user does not start these processes. Prefer `analyzeHeadless` for unattended work; use MCP only when a Ghidra GUI bridge is already configured. The agent tries `ghidra-mcp-up --launch` first and falls back to headless when no launcher exists.

## Start

```powershell
# Agent path: wait for both layers when a launcher is configured.
ghidra-mcp-up --launch

# Fallback, only when a GhidraMCP bridge is installed:
# 1. Ghidra with the GhidraMCP extension installed; open the target project.
#    Keep the Ghidra GUI responsive: do not open modal dialogs while indexing.

# 2. Start the MCP bridge (uvx resolves the package).
uvx --from git+https://github.com/LaurieWired/GhidraMCP ghidra-mcp `
  --transport streamable-http --mcp-host 127.0.0.1 --mcp-port 8081 `
  --ghidra-server http://127.0.0.1:8080/
```

## Verify

```powershell
curl.exe -s http://127.0.0.1:8080/methods | Select-Object -First 1
curl.exe -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8081/mcp `
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" `
  -H "Accept: application/json, text/event-stream" `
  -d '{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":1,\"method\":\"initialize\",\"params\":{\"protocolVersion\":\"2025-03-26\",\"capabilities\":{},\"clientInfo\":{\"name\":\"reverse-this\",\"version\":\"1\"}}}'
```

The kit's `ghidra-mcp-up.cmd` probes both ports and prints the exact failing layer. It is optional: `analyzeHeadless` needs no server.

## Operating rules

1. **List before inspect.** Call `list_project_files` first; the active program may not be the one you assume.
2. **Wait for indexing.** After opening a large binary, `get_function_count` and similar calls can lag. Retry with a bounded backoff (1 s, 2 s, 4 s, 8 s); do not hammer the bridge.
3. **No GUI modals.** A modal dialog blocks the Swing thread and the HTTP bridge stops answering. Close dialogs before calling tools; if the bridge times out, check the Ghidra window first.
4. **One program per call.** Pass the program name/path explicitly when the project has more than one.
5. **Batch reads, not writes.** Renames, comments and type changes are separate, auditable calls; do not script a bulk mutation without a backup of the project.
6. **Record the evidence.** For every finding, keep the function address, the decompiled excerpt and the tool call that produced it.

## Failure map

| Symptom | Check | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `8081` connection refused | Bridge not started | Start `ghidra-mcp` with `--transport streamable-http` |
| `8080` connection refused | Ghidra plugin not loaded | Enable the GhidraMCP extension and reopen the project |
| Calls hang | GUI modal or long analysis | Close the dialog, wait for analysis, retry |
| Empty function list | Wrong program active | `list_project_files`, then select the program |
| 406 on `POST /mcp` | Missing `Accept: text/event-stream` | Send both JSON and SSE in `Accept` |

```

if not "%result%"=="0" (

  echo Modkit install reported failures. See sandbox\log\modkit.log
  if "%~1"=="" pause
  exit /b %result%
)
echo.
echo Modkit command completed.
if "%~1"=="" pause
exit /b %result%
